\quantity(\frac{2}{n}, \frac{20\gamma_{t-1}}{n})
-Bernstein condition, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}\E_{t-1}\quantity[\tnpt_\tau\indicator_{\tnpt_\tau> L}]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}\Pr_{t-1}\gamma_{t}>\quantity[\gamma_t> \E_{t-1}[\gamma_t]+\xgamma]_{t-1}[\gamma_{t}]+x_{\gamma}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}\exp\left(-\frac{x_{\gamma}^{2}/2}{\frac{20\gamma_{t-1}}{n}+\frac{2x_{\gamma}}{3n}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}\exp\quantity(-\Omega(n\xgamma))

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}}{24n}.

Consequently,

\displaystyle\E\quantity[\tnpt_\tau]

\displaystyle\leq L+\sum_{t=1}^{\infty}\E\quantity[\frac{\indicator_{\tau>t-1}}{100n}]=55x_{\gamma}+\frac{\E[\tau]}{24n}.

Thus, we have

\displaystyle\E[\tau]\leq 12\E[\widetilde{\gamma}_{\tau}]\leq 12n55x_{\gamma}+\frac{\quantity(55\xgamma+\frac{\E[\tau]}{24n})[\tau]}{24n}\leq 660nx_{\gamma}+\frac{\E[\tau]}{2},

i.e.,
\E[\tau]\leq 1320nx_{\gamma}
.
∎

Proof of
Lemma

5.4
.

Let
\tau=\min\{\tau_{\gamma}^{+},\tau_{\beta}^{-},\tau_{\psi}^{+}\}
and
T^{\prime}=1320\mathrm{e}n^{2}x_{\gamma}
.
From the Markov inequality and
Lemma

5.5
,

\displaystyle\Pr\quantity[\tau>T']\leq\frac{\E[\tau]}{T^{\prime}}\leq\frac{1}{\mathrm{e}}.

Hence, for any
\ell\geq 1
, the Markov property implies that

\displaystyle\Pr\quantity[\tau>\ell T' \mid\tau> (\ell-1) T']

\displaystyle=\E\quantity[\Pr_{(\ell-1)T'}\qty[\tau>\ell T'] \mid\tau> (\ell-1) T']\leq\frac{1}{\mathrm{e}}.

Thus, we obtain

\displaystyle\Pr\quantity[\tau>\ell T']

\displaystyle=\Pr\quantity[\tau>\ell T' \mid\tau> (\ell-1) T']\Pr\quantity[\tau> (\ell-1) T']\leq\frac{1}{\mathrm{e}}\Pr\quantity[\tau> (\ell-1) T']\leq\cdots\leq\frac{1}{\mathrm{e}^{\ell}}.

Applying
Lemma

5.2
(
Item

3
) and
Lemma

5.3
(
Item

2
), we obtain

\displaystyle\Pr\quantity[\taugammaplus> \ell T' \text{ or } \min\{\taubetaminus,\taupsiplus\}\leq\ell T']

\displaystyle\leq\Pr\quantity[\qty{\taugammaplus> \ell T' \text{ or } \min\{\taubetaminus,\taupsiplus\}\leq\ell T'} \text{ and } \min\{\taubetaminus,\taupsiplus\} > \ell T']+\Pr\quantity[\min\{\taubetaminus,\taupsiplus\}\leq\ell T']

\displaystyle\leq\Pr\quantity[\tau>\ell T']+\Pr\quantity[\taubetaminus\leq\ell T'\text{ and } \taupsiplus> \ell T']+\Pr\quantity[\taupsiplus\leq\ell T']

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\mathrm{e}^{\ell}}+\ell T^{\prime}\exp\quantity(-\Omega\qty(n\xbeta^2))+\ell T^{\prime}\exp\quantity(-\Omega\qty(n\xpsi^2)).

Taking
\ell=C\log n
for a sufficiently large constant
C>0
, we obtain the claim.
∎

5.4
Behavior of Maximum Population

In this subsection, we track the change of the maximum fractional population
\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t}=\max_{i\in[k]}\alpha_{t}(i)
and its normalized version
\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{t}=\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t}/\beta_{t}
.
Note that, from
Lemma

5.4
, we know that
\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t}=\Omega(\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{t})
becomes
\Omega((\log n)^{2}/\sqrt{n})
within
\widetilde{O}(\sqrt{n})
rounds with high probability.
The aim of this subsection is to show that, with high probability,
(i)
\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{t}\geq(1-c^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}})\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}
holds for all
t\leq O(n\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}/\log n)
(see
Lemma

5.6
,
Item

1
), and
(ii)
\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t}\leq(1+c_{\max}^{\uparrow})\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}
holds for all
t\leq O(1/\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0})
(see
Lemma

5.6
,
Item

2
).

Lemma 5.6

(Key properties of
\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{t}
and
\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t}
)
.

Suppose that
\psi_{0}\leq x_{\psi}
and
\beta_{0}\geq 1/2-x_{\beta}
.
We have the following:

1.

Let
C_{\mbox{\tiny\ref{lem:hitting time for tnpm and npm}(\ref{item:tildemaxdown})}}=c^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}}/36
be a positive constant. Then, for any
T\leq C_{\mbox{\tiny\ref{lem:hitting time for tnpm and npm}(\ref{item:tildemaxdown})}}n
,

\displaystyle\Pr\quantity[\tautildemaxdown\le T \text{ and } \min\{\taubetaminus,\taupsiplus\}> T]\leq T\exp\quantity(-\Omega\qty(\frac{\tnpm_0n}{T})).

2.

Suppose
\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}=\omega(\log n/\sqrt{n})
.
Let
C_{\mbox{\tiny\ref{lem:hitting time for tnpm and npm}(\ref{item:taualphamaxup})}}=\frac{c_{\max}^{\uparrow}}{6(1+c_{\max}^{\uparrow})^{2}}
be a positive constant.
Then, for any
T\leq\frac{C_{\mbox{\tiny\ref{lem:hitting time for tnpm and npm}(\ref{item:taualphamaxup})}}}{\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}}
,

\displaystyle\Pr\quantity[\taumaxup\leq\min\{T,\taubetaminus\}]\leq k\exp\quantity(-\Omega\qty(\frac{\npm_0n}{T})).

First, we introduce the following key lemma for the proof of
Lemma

5.6
(
Item

1
).

Lemma 5.7
.

Suppose that
\psi_{0}\leq x_{\psi}
and
\beta_{0}\geq 1/2-x_{\beta}
.
Let
\tau^{\uparrow}=\max\{\tau_{\max}^{\uparrow},\tau^{\uparrow}_{\widetilde{\max}}\}
.
Let
C_{\mbox{\tiny\ref{lem:hitting time for tnpm and npm}(\ref{item:tildemaxdown})}}=c^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}}/36
be a positive constant defined in
Lemma

5.6
(
Item

1
).
Then, for any
T\leq C_{\mbox{\tiny\ref{lem:hitting time for tnpm and npm}(\ref{item:tildemaxdown})}}n
,

\displaystyle\Pr\quantity[\tautildemaxdown\leq\min\{T,\tau^\uparrow,\taupsiplus,\taubetaminus\}]\leq\exp\quantity(-\Omega\qty(\frac{\tnpm_0n}{T})).

Proof of
Lemma

5.7
.

Let
\tau^{\uparrow}=\max\{\tau_{\max}^{\uparrow},\tau^{\uparrow}_{\widetilde{\max}}\}

and
\tau=\min\{\tau^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}},\tau^{\uparrow},\tau_{\beta}^{-},\tau_{\psi}^{+}\}
.
For
\tau>t-1
,

Item

1
of
Lemma

4.7
yields that

\displaystyle\E_{t-1}\quantity[\tnpm_t]\geq\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}\quantity(1+\frac{\npm_{t-1}-\npt_{t-1}/\npo_{t-1}}{2(1-\npo_{t-1})+\npt_{t-1}/\npo_{t-1}})-\frac{\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}}{9n}\geq\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}-\frac{18\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}}{n}.

Note that for
\tau^{\uparrow}>t-1
, we have either
\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}\leq(1+c_{\max}^{\uparrow})\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}\leq 2\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}
or
\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}\leq(1+c^{\uparrow}_{\widetilde{\max}})\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}\beta_{t-1}\leq 2\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}
, so in either case,
\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}\leq 2\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}
holds whenever
\tau^{\uparrow}>t-1
.

Hence, letting
X_{t}=\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{t}
and
R=-\frac{18\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}}{n}<0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}X_{t-1}+R-\quantity(X_{t-1}+\drift-\E_{t-1}[X_t])_{t-1}[X_{t}]=\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}-\frac{18\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}}{n}-\quantity(\tnpm_{t-1}-\frac{18\tnpm_{0}}{n}-\E_{t-1}[\tnpm_t])_{t-1}[\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{t}]\leq 0.

Furthermore,

\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}\quantity(X_{t-1}+\drift-X_t)\leq\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}\quantity(\tnpm_{t-1}\qty(1+\frac{\npm_{t-1}-\npt_{t-1}/\npo_{t-1}}{2(1-\npo_{t-1})+\npt_{t-1}/\npo_{t-1}})-\frac{9\npm_{t-1}}{n}-\tnpm_t)

satisfies one-sided
\quantity(O\qty(\frac{1}{n}),O\qty(\frac{\npm_0}{n}))
-Bernstein condition.
Note that we use
Lemma

4.7
(
Item

2
) and
Lemma

3.7
(
Items

2
and
3
).

Applying
Lemma

3.11
(
Item

1
) with
I^{-}=(1-c^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}})\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}
,
I^{+}=(1+c^{\uparrow}_{\widetilde{\max}})\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}
, and
T\leq C_{\mbox{\tiny\ref{lem:hitting time for tnpm and npm}(\ref{item:tildemaxdown})}}n=\frac{c^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}}}{36}n=\frac{X_{0}-I^{-}}{-2R}
, we have

\displaystyle\Pr\quantity[\tautildemaxdown\leq\min\{T,\tau^\uparrow,\taubetaminus,\taupsiplus\}]\leq\exp\quantity(-\Omega\qty(\frac{(\tnpm_0)^2}{\frac{\tnpm_0}{n}T+\frac{\tnpm_0}{n}}))\leq\exp\quantity(- \Omega\qty(\frac{\tnpm_0 n}{T})).

∎

Proof of
Lemma

5.6
(
Item

1
).

For
s\geq 0
, let
\tau_{s}^{\downarrow}=\inf\{t\geq s:\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{t}\leq(1-c^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}})\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{s}\}
and
\tau_{s}^{\uparrow}=\inf\{t\geq s:\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{t}\geq(1+c^{\uparrow}_{\widetilde{\max}})\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{s}\}
.
Applying
Lemma

A.7
(
Item

1
) with
\tau^{*}=\min\{\tau_{\beta}^{-},\tau_{\psi}^{+}\}
and
T\leq C_{\mbox{\tiny\ref{lem:hitting time for tnpm and npm}(\ref{item:tildemaxdown})}}n
, we have

\displaystyle\Pr\quantity[\tautildemaxdown\leq T \text{ and } \tau^*> T]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s=0}^{T-1}\E\quantity[\indicator_{\tnpm_s\geq\tnpm_0 \text{ and } \tau^*> s}\Pr_s\qty[\tau_s^\downarrow\leq\min\{T,\tau_s^\uparrow,\tau^*\}]]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s=0}^{T-1}\E\quantity[\indicator_{\tnpm_s\geq\tnpm_0 \text{ and } \tau^*> s}\exp\qty(-\Omega\qty(\frac{\tnpm_sn}{T}))]

\displaystyle\leq T\exp\quantity(-\Omega\qty(\frac{\tnpm_0n}{T})).

Note that we apply
Lemma

5.7
in the second inequality.
∎

Proof of
Lemma

5.6
(
Item

2
).

Let
\tau=\min\{\tau_{\max}^{\uparrow},\tau_{\beta}^{-}\}
.
For
\tau>T
, we have

\displaystyle\E_{t-1}[\alpha_{t}(i)]-\alpha_{t-1}(i)

\displaystyle=\alpha_{t-1}(i)\quantity(\alpha_{t-1}(i)+1-2\beta_{t-1})

\displaystyle\leq\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}\quantity(\npm_{t-1}+2\xbeta)

\displaystyle\leq 3(1+c_{\max}^{\uparrow})^{2}(\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0})^{2}.

(13)

Note that we use assumptions on
\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}=\omega(\log n/\sqrt{n})
and
x_{\beta}=o(\log n/\sqrt{n})
in the second inequality.

Hence, letting
X_{t}=-\alpha_{t}(i)
and
R=-3(1+c_{\max}^{\uparrow})^{2}(\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0})^{2}<0
, we have

\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}X_{t-1}+R-\quantity(X_{t-1}+\drift-\E_{t-1}[X_t])_{t-1}[X_{t}]=\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}\quantity(\E_{t-1}[\alpha_t(i)]-\alpha_{t-1}(i)-3(1+\cmaxup)^2(\npm_{0})^2)_{t-1}[\alpha_{t}(i)]-\alpha_{t-1}(i)-3(1+c_{\max}^{\uparrow})^{2}(\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0})^{2}\leq 0

and
\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}\quantity(\E_{t-1}[X_t]-X_t)_{t-1}[X_{t}]-X_{t}=\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}\alpha_{t}(i)-\quantity(\alpha_t(i)-\E_{t-1}[\alpha_t(i)])_{t-1}[\alpha_{t}(i)]
satisfies
\quantity(1/n,O(\npm_0/n))
-Bernstein condition.
Note that we use
Lemma

4.2
(
Item

3
) and
Lemma

3.7
(
Items

2
and
3
).

Let
\tau_{i}=\inf\{t\geq 0:\alpha_{t}(i)\geq(1+c_{\max}^{\uparrow})\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}\}=\inf\{t\geq 0:X_{t}\leq-(1+c_{\max}^{\uparrow})\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}\}
.
Applying
Lemma

3.11
(
Item

1
) with

I^{-}=-(1+c_{\max}^{\uparrow})\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}
, for
T\leq\frac{C_{\mbox{\tiny\ref{lem:hitting time for tnpm and npm}(\ref{item:taualphamaxup})}}}{\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}}=\frac{c_{\max}^{\uparrow}}{6(1+c_{\max}^{\uparrow})^{2}\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}}=\frac{X_{0}-I^{-}}{-2R}
, we have

\displaystyle\Pr\quantity[\tau_i\leq\min\{T,\tau\}]\leq\exp\quantity(-\Omega\qty(\frac{(\npm_0)^2}{\frac{\npm_0}{n}T+\frac{\npm_0}{n}}))\leq\exp\quantity(-\Omega\qty(\frac{\npm_0 n}{T})).

Thus, applying the union bound,

\displaystyle\Pr\quantity[\taumaxup\leq\min\{T,\tau\}]

\displaystyle\leq\Pr\quantity[\exists i\in[k]: \tau_i\leq\min\{T,\tau\}]\leq k\exp\quantity(-\Omega\qty(\frac{\npm_0 n}{T})).

∎

Finally, as a natural consequence of the discussion so far, we introduce the following useful lemma.

Lemma 5.8
.

Suppose that
\beta_{0}\geq 1/2-x_{\beta}
and
\psi_{0}\leq x_{\psi}
. Then, for any
T\leq C_{\mbox{\tiny\ref{lem:hitting time for tnpm and npm}(\ref{item:tildemaxdown})}}n
,

\displaystyle\Pr\quantity[\min\{\tautildemaxdown,\taubetaminus,\taupsiplus\} \leq T]\leq T\exp\quantity(-\Omega\qty(\frac{n\tnpm_0}{T}))+T\exp\quantity(-\Omega(n\xpsi^2)).

Proof.

First, we observe that, for any stopping times
\tau_{1}
and
\tau_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\Pr\quantity[\min\{\tau_1,\tau_2\}\leq T]

\displaystyle\leq\Pr\quantity[\{\tau_1 \leq T \text{ or } \tau_2\leq T\}\text{ and } \tau_2>T]+\Pr\quantity[\tau_2\leq T]

\displaystyle=\Pr\quantity[\tau_1 \leq T \text{ and } \tau_2>T]+\Pr\quantity[\tau_2\leq T].

(14)

Hence, combining
Lemma

5.6
(
Item

1
),
Lemma

5.2
, and
Lemma

5.3
,

\displaystyle\Pr\quantity[\min\{\tautildemaxdown,\taubetaminus,\taupsiplus\} \leq T]

\displaystyle\leq\Pr\quantity[\tautildemaxdown\leq T \text{ and }\min\{\taubetaminus,\taupsiplus\}>T]+\Pr\quantity[\taubetaminus\leq T \text{ and }\taupsiplus>T]+\Pr\quantity[\taupsiplus\leq T]

\displaystyle\leq T\exp\quantity(-\Omega\qty(\frac{n\tnpm_0}{T}))+T\exp\quantity(-\Omega\qty(n\xbeta^2))+T\exp\quantity(-\Omega\qty(n\xpsi^2)).

∎

5.5
Behavior of Gap between Two Opinions

For two opinions
i,j\in[k]
, recall
\delta_{t}=\delta_{t}(i,j)=\alpha_{t}(i)-\alpha_{t}(j)
is the gap between the two opinions at time
t
(
Definition

4.1
).
We define the
weak
opinion and its stopping time as follows:

Definition 5.9

(Weak Opinion)
.

For a constant
c_{\mathrm{weak}}\in(0,1/2)
and an opinion
i\in[k]
, define

\displaystyle\tau_{i}^{\mathrm{weak}}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\inf\{t\geq 0:\alpha_{t}(i)\leq(1-c_{\mathrm{weak}})\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t}\}.

By default, we set
c_{\mathrm{weak}}=0.1
.
We call that an opinion
i\in[k]
is
weak
at time
t
if
\alpha_{t}(i)\leq(1-c_{\mathrm{weak}})\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t}
.

The main results of this section are the following two lemmas.
Intuitively, they show that if both
i
and
j
are non-weak, then:
(i) the gap
\delta_{t}(i,j)
between them grows to at least
\Omega(\sqrt{\log n/n})
within
O(\log n/\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0})
rounds (
Lemma

5.10
(
Item

1
)), and
(ii) if the initial gap
\delta_{0}(i,j)
is at least
\Omega(\sqrt{\log n/n})
, then
j
becomes weak within
O(\log n/\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0})
rounds (
Lemma

5.10
(
Item

2
)).

Lemma 5.10

(Either of two non-weak opinions becomes weak)
.

Let
i,j\in[k]
be an arbitrary pair of two non-weak opinions.
Suppose that
\beta_{0}\geq 1/2-x_{\beta}
,
\psi_{0}\leq x_{\psi}
, and
\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}\geq\omega(\log n/\sqrt{n})
.
We have the following:

1.

Let
C
be an arbitrary positive constant.
Then, for some
T=O(\log n/\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0})
, we have

\displaystyle\Pr\quantity[\min\qty{\taudeltaplus\qty(\sqrt{\frac{C\log n}{n}}),\tauiweak,\taujweak} > T \text{ or } \min\{\tautildemaxdown,\taubetaminus,\taupsiplus\} \leq T]\leq n^{-10}.

2.

Suppose
\delta_{0}(i,j)\geq\sqrt{C\log n/n}
for a sufficiently large constant
C>0
.
Then, for some
T=O\quantity(\log n/\npm_0)
,

\displaystyle\Pr\quantity[ \taujweak>T \text{ or } \min\{\tautildemaxdown,\taubetaminus,\taupsiplus\} \leq T ]\leq n^{-10}.

To this end, we begin with proving that
\delta_{t}
employs both multiplicative and additive drifts as long as both
i
and
j
are non-weak.

Lemma 5.11

(Multiplicative and additive drifts of
\delta_{t}
)
.

Let
i,j\in[k]
be an arbitrary pair of two non-weak opinions.
Suppose
\psi_{0}\leq x_{\psi}
,
\beta_{0}\geq 1/2-x_{\beta}
, and
\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}=\omega(\log n/\sqrt{n})
.
Let
C_{\mbox{\tiny\ref{lem:hitting time for tnpm and npm}(\ref{item:taualphamaxup})}}=\frac{c_{\max}^{\uparrow}}{6(1+c_{\max}^{\uparrow})^{2}}
be a positive constant defined in
Lemma

5.6
(
Item

2
).
We have the following:

1.

Suppose
\delta_{0}(i,j)\geq 0
.
Let
c_{\delta}^{\uparrow}=C_{\mbox{\tiny\ref{lem:hitting time for tnpm and npm}(\ref{item:taualphamaxup})}}\frac{(1-c^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}})\quantity(1-2\cweak)(1-c_{\delta}^{\downarrow})}{12}
.
Then,

\displaystyle\Pr\quantity[\min\{\tau_\delta^\uparrow,\taujweak,\tautildemaxdown,\taubetaminus,\taupsiplus\} >\frac{\constrefs{lem:hitting time for tnpm and npm}{item:taualphamaxup}}{\npm_0}]\ \leq\exp\quantity(-\Omega\qty(n\delta_0(i,j)^2))+n\exp\quantity(-\Omega\qty(n(\npm_0)^2)).

2.

Let
x_{\delta}=c_{\delta}^{+}/\sqrt{n}
for
c_{\delta}^{+}=\frac{c_{\max}^{\uparrow}(1-2c_{\mathrm{weak}})^{2}(1-c^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}})}{6\cdot 96\cdot 64(1+c_{\max}^{\uparrow})^{2}(1-c_{\mathrm{weak}})}
.
Then, there exists a positive constant
c_{*}\in(0,1)
such that

\displaystyle\Pr\quantity[\min\qty{\taudeltaplus,\tauiweak,\taujweak,\tautildemaxdown,\taubetaminus,\taupsiplus} > \frac{\constrefs{lem:hitting time for tnpm and npm}{item:taualphamaxup}}{\npm_0}]\leq 1-c_{*}.

Multiplicative Drift: Proof of
Lemma

5.11
(
Item

1
).

This part is devoted to proving that
\delta_{t}
grows by a constant factor within
O(1/\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0})
rounds.

Proof of
Lemma

5.11
(
Item

1
).

Let
\tau^{*}=\min\{\tau_{j}^{\mathrm{weak}},\tau_{\max}^{\uparrow},\tau^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}},\tau_{\beta}^{-},\tau_{\psi}^{+}\}
and
\tau=\min\{\tau_{\delta}^{\uparrow},\tau_{\delta}^{\downarrow},\tau^{*}\}
.
For
\tau>t-1
, we have

\displaystyle\beta_{t-1}\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}\quantity(\frac{\alpha_{t-1}(i)+\alpha_{t-1}(j)}{\npm_{t-1}}-\frac{\gamma_{t-1}}{\beta_{t-1}\npm_{t-1}}-\frac{\psi_{t-1}}{\beta_{t-1}^2\tnpm_{t-1}})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1-c^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}}}{3}\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}\quantity(2(1-\cweak)-1-\underbrace{\frac{\xpsi}{(1/3)^2(1-\ctildemaxdown)\tnpm_{0}}}_{o(1)})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{(1-c^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}})(1-2c_{\mathrm{weak}})}{6}\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}.

(15)

Note that
\gamma_{t-1}\leq\beta_{t-1}\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}
and
\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}\geq\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}=\omega(\sqrt{\log n/n})
.
Hence, for
\tau>t-1
, we have

\displaystyle\E_{t-1}[\delta_{t}]

\displaystyle=\delta_{t-1}+\delta_{t-1}\beta_{t-1}\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}\quantity(\frac{\alpha_{t-1}(i)+\alpha_{t-1}(j)}{\npm_{t-1}}-\frac{\gamma_{t-1}}{\beta_{t-1}\npm_{t-1}}-\frac{\psi_{t-1}}{\beta_{t-1}^2\tnpm_{t-1}})

\displaystyle\geq\delta_{t-1}+\frac{(1-c^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}})(1-c_{\delta}^{\downarrow})(1-2c_{\mathrm{weak}})}{6}\delta_{0}\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}.

Note that we use
Lemma

4.4
(
Item

1
).

Letting
X_{t}=\delta_{t}
and
R=\frac{(1-c^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}})(1-c_{\delta}^{\downarrow})(1-2c_{\mathrm{weak}})}{6}\delta_{0}\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}>0
, we have

\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}X_{t-1}+R-\quantity(X_{t-1}+\drift-\E_{t-1}[X_t])_{t-1}[X_{t}]=\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}\delta_{t-1}+R-\quantity(\delta_{t-1}+\drift-\E_{t-1}[\delta_t])_{t-1}[\delta_{t}]\leq 0

and
\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}\quantity(\E_{t-1}[X_t]-X_t)_{t-1}[X_{t}]-X_{t}=\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}\quantity(\E_{t-1}[\delta_t]-\delta_t)_{t-1}[\delta_{t}]-\delta_{t}
satisfies
\quantity(O(1/n),O(\npm_0/n))
-Bernstein condition.
Note that we use
Lemma

4.4
(
Item

3
) and
Lemma

3.7
(
Items

2
and
3
).

Recall
c_{\delta}^{\uparrow}=C_{\mbox{\tiny\ref{lem:hitting time for tnpm and npm}(\ref{item:taualphamaxup})}}\frac{(1-c^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}})\quantity(1-2\cweak)(1-c_{\delta}^{\downarrow})}{12}
.
Let
I^{+}=(1+c)\delta_{0}
and
I^{-}=(1-c)\delta_{0}
.
Let
T=\frac{C_{\mbox{\tiny\ref{lem:hitting time for tnpm and npm}(\ref{item:taualphamaxup})}}}{\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}}
.
We have
T=\frac{12c_{\delta}^{\uparrow}}{(1-c^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}})\quantity(1-2\cweak)(1-c_{\delta}^{\downarrow})}\cdot\frac{1}{\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}}=\frac{2(I^{+}-\delta_{0})}{R}
.
Thus, we can apply
Lemma

3.11
(
Item

2
) and obtain

\displaystyle\Pr\quantity[\min\{\tau_\delta^\uparrow,\tau^*\} >T]\ \leq\exp\quantity(-\Omega\qty(n\delta_0^2)).

(16)

Applying
Lemma

5.6
(
Item

2
), we have

\displaystyle\Pr\quantity[\min\{\taudeltaup,\taujweak,\tautildemaxdown,\taupsiplus\}>T \text{ and } \taubetaminus>T]

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\Pr\quantity[\min\{\taudeltaup,\taujweak,\tautildemaxdown,\taupsiplus\}>T \text{ and } \taubetaminus>T \text{ and } \taumaxup>T]}_{\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:taudeltaup lemma}}+\underbrace{\Pr\quantity[\taumaxup\leq T \text{ and } \taubetaminus>T]}_{\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:hitting time for tnpm and npm}(\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{item:taualphamaxup})}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\quantity(-\Omega\qty(n\delta_0^2))+n\exp\quantity(-\Omega(n(\npm_0)^2)).

∎

Additive Drift: Proof of
Lemma

5.11
(
Item

2
).

Next, we prove that
\absolutevalue{\delta_t}
grows at least
\Omega(1/\sqrt{n})
within
O(1/\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0})
rounds with constant probability.
To prove
Lemma

5.11
(
Item

2
), we first prove the following lemma.

Lemma 5.12
.

Suppose that
\beta_{0}\geq 1/2-x_{\beta}
,
\psi_{0}\leq x_{\psi}
, and
\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}\geq\omega(\log n/\sqrt{n})
.
Let
\tau=\min\{\tau_{\delta}^{+},\tau_{i}^{\mathrm{weak}},\tau_{j}^{\mathrm{weak}},\tau^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}},\tau_{\beta}^{-},\tau_{\psi}^{+}\}
.
Let
C_{\mbox{\tiny\ref{lem:taudeltaplus}}}=\frac{(1-2c_{\mathrm{weak}})^{2}(1-c^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}})}{96(1-c_{\mathrm{weak}})}
.
Then, we have

\displaystyle\E[\tau]\leq\frac{n\E[\delta_{\tau}^{2}]}{C_{\mbox{\tiny\ref{lem:taudeltaplus}}}\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}}.

Proof.

First, for
\min\{\tau_{i}^{\mathrm{weak}},\tau_{j}^{\mathrm{weak}}\}>t-1
, we observe that

\displaystyle\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}\leq\frac{\min\{\alpha_{t-1}(i),\alpha_{t-1}(j)\}}{1-c_{\mathrm{weak}}}\leq\frac{1}{2(1-c_{\mathrm{weak}})}

(17)

holds.
Hence, for
\min\{\tau_{\beta}^{-},\tau_{\psi}^{+},\tau_{i}^{\mathrm{weak}},\tau_{j}^{\mathrm{weak}}\}>t-1
, we have

\displaystyle 1-\beta_{t-1}=\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\psi_{t-1}+\gamma_{t-1}}{2\beta_{t-1}}\geq\frac{1}{2}-6x_{\psi}-\frac{\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}}{2}\geq\frac{1}{2}-6x_{\psi}-\frac{1}{4(1-c_{\mathrm{weak}})}\geq\frac{1-2c_{\mathrm{weak}}}{8(1-c_{\mathrm{weak}})}.

Note that we use our assumption of
x_{\psi}=o(1)
.
Further, for
\min\{\tau_{\beta}^{-},\tau_{\psi}^{+},\tau_{i}^{\mathrm{weak}},\tau_{j}^{\mathrm{weak}}\}>t-1
,

\displaystyle\alpha_{t-1}(i)+\alpha_{t-1}(j)+1-2\beta_{t-1}

\displaystyle=\beta_{t-1}\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}\quantity(\frac{\alpha_{t-1}(i)+\alpha_{t-1}(j)}{\npm_{t-1}}-\frac{\gamma_{t-1}}{\beta_{t-1}\npm_{t-1}}-\frac{\psi_{t-1}}{\beta_{t-1}^2\tnpm_{t-1}})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1-c^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}}}{3}\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}\quantity(2(1-\cweak)-1-\underbrace{\frac{\xpsi}{(1/3)^2(1-\ctildemaxdown)\tnpm_{0}}}_{o(1)})\geq 0.

(18)

Note that
\gamma_{t-1}\leq\beta_{t-1}\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}
and
\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}\geq\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}=\omega(\sqrt{\log n/n})
.

Let
\tau=\min\{\tau_{\delta}^{+},\tau_{i}^{\mathrm{weak}},\tau_{j}^{\mathrm{weak}},\tau^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}},\tau_{\beta}^{-},\tau_{\psi}^{+}\}
.
For
t-1<\tau
,

\displaystyle\E_{t-1}[\delta_{t}^{2}]

\displaystyle=\E_{t-1}[\delta_{t}]^{2}+\Var_{t-1}[\delta_{t}]

\displaystyle\geq\delta_{t-1}^{2}\quantity(1+\alpha_{t-1}(i)+\alpha_{t-1}(j)+1-2\beta_{t-1})^{2}+\frac{(1-\beta_{t-1})^{2}}{n}(\alpha_{t-1}(i)+\alpha_{t-1}(j))

\displaystyle\geq\delta_{t-1}^{2}+\frac{(1-2c_{\mathrm{weak}})^{2}}{32(1-c_{\mathrm{weak}})n}\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}

\displaystyle\geq\delta_{t-1}^{2}+\frac{(1-2c_{\mathrm{weak}})^{2}(1-c^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}})}{96(1-c_{\mathrm{weak}})}\cdot\frac{\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}}{n}.

Note that we use
Lemma

4.4
(
Items

1
and
2
).

Let

R=C_{\mbox{\tiny\ref{lem:taudeltaplus}}}\frac{\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}}{n}
,

X_{t}=\delta_{t}^{2}-Rt
and
Y_{t}=X_{t\wedge\tau}
.
Then, we have

\displaystyle\E_{t-1}[Y_{t}-Y_{t-1}]=\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}\E_{t-1}[X_{t}-X_{t-1}]=\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}\quantity(\E_{t-1}[\delta_t^2]-\drift t-\delta_{t-1}^2+\drift(t-1))_{t-1}[\delta_{t}^{2}]-Rt-\delta_{t-1}^{2}+R(t-1)\geq 0,

i.e.,
(Y_{t})_{t\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
is a submartingale.
From
Theorem

A.4
, we have

\displaystyle\E[Y_{\tau}]\geq\E[Y_{0}]=\delta_{0}^{2}\geq 0

and

\displaystyle\E[Y_{\tau}]=\E[X_{\tau}]=\E[\delta_{\tau}^{2}]-R\E[\tau].

Thus, we obtain
\E[\tau]\leq\frac{\E[\delta_{\tau}^{2}]}{R}
.
∎

Lemma 5.13
.

Let
c\in(0,1)
be an arbitrary constant and let
x_{\delta}=c/\sqrt{n}
.
Let
C_{\mbox{\tiny\ref{lem:taudeltaplus bounded jump}}}
be a sufficiently large positive constant.
Suppose
\psi_{0}\leq x_{\psi}
and
\beta_{0}\geq 1/2-x_{\beta}
.
Suppose
\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}=\omega(\log n/\sqrt{n})
and
\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}\leq 16c^{2}/C_{\mbox{\tiny\ref{lem:taudeltaplus bounded jump}}}
.
Let
\tau=\min\{\tau_{\delta}^{+},\tau_{\beta}^{-},\tau_{\psi}^{+},\tau^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}},\tau_{i}^{\mathrm{weak}},\tau_{j}^{\mathrm{weak}},\tau_{\max}^{\uparrow}\}
.
Then,

\E[\delta_{\tau}^{2}]\leq 16x_{\delta}^{2}+\frac{C_{\mbox{\tiny\ref{lem:taudeltaplus}}}\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}\E[\tau]}{2n},

where
C_{\mbox{\tiny\ref{lem:taudeltaplus}}}
is the constant defined in
Lemma

5.12
.

Proof.

Write
L=16c^{2}/n
and
R=C_{\mbox{\tiny\ref{lem:taudeltaplus}}}\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}/n
.
We have

\displaystyle\E[\delta_{\tau}^{2}]

\displaystyle=\E[\mathbf{1}_{\delta_{\tau}^{2}\leq L}]+\E[\mathbf{1}_{\delta_{\tau}^{2}>L}]\leq L+\E[\mathbf{1}_{\delta_{\tau}^{2}>L}]

and

\displaystyle\E[\mathbf{1}_{\delta_{\tau}^{2}>L}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{\infty}\E[\mathbf{1}_{\tau=t}\delta_{t}^{2}\mathbf{1}_{\delta_{t}^{2}>L}]\leq\sum_{t=1}^{\infty}\E[\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}\delta_{t}^{2}\mathbf{1}_{\delta_{t}^{2}>L}]=\sum_{t=1}^{\infty}\E\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}\quantity[\indicator_{\tau>t-1}\E_{t-1}[\delta_t^2\indicator_{\delta_t^2>L}]]_{t-1}[\delta_{t}^{2}\mathbf{1}_{\delta_{t}^{2}>L}].

In the following, we show

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}\E_{t-1}[\delta_{t}^{2}\mathbf{1}_{\delta_{t}^{2}>L}]\leq\frac{R}{2}.

(19)

Note that by establishing this inequality, we have

\E[\delta_{\tau}^{2}]\leq L+\frac{R}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{\infty}\E[\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}]=L+\frac{R}{2}\E[\tau],

which proves the claim.

First, we observe that

\displaystyle\E_{t-1}[\delta_{t}^{2}\mathbf{1}_{\delta_{t}^{2}>L}]

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\Pr_{t-1}[\delta_{t}^{2}\mathbf{1}_{\delta_{t}^{2}>L}>y]dy=\int_{0}^{1}\Pr_{t-1}[\delta_{t}^{2}>(y\vee L)]dy=\int_{0}^{1}\Pr_{t-1}\quantity[\abs{\delta_t}>\sqrt{y\vee L}]dy

holds.
For
\tau_{\delta}^{+}>t-1
, we have

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{\E_{t-1}[\delta_t]}_{t-1}[\delta_{t}]

\displaystyle=\absolutevalue{\delta_{t-1}\qty(\alpha_{t-1}(i)+\alpha_{t-1}(j)+2(1-\beta_{t-1}))}\leq 2\absolutevalue{\delta_{t-1}}\leq 2x_{\delta}\leq\frac{\sqrt{L}}{2}\leq\frac{\sqrt{y\vee L}}{2}.

Hence, for
\tau>t-1
, we have

\displaystyle\Pr_{t-1}\quantity[\abs{\delta_t}>\sqrt{y\vee L}]

\displaystyle\leq\Pr_{t-1}\quantity[\abs{\delta_t-\E_{t-1}[\delta_t]}+\frac{\sqrt{y\vee L}}{2}>\sqrt{y\vee L}]+\frac{\sqrt{y\vee L}}{2}>\sqrt{y\vee L}

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\quantity(-\frac{(y\vee L)/4}{\frac{2(\alpha_{t-1}(i)+\alpha_{t-1}(j))}{n}+\frac{\sqrt{y\vee L}}{3n}})

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\quantity(-\frac{(y\vee L)n/4}{4(1+\cmaxup)\npm_0+\sqrt{y\vee L}/3})

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\quantity(-\frac{(y\vee L)n}{32(1+\cmaxup)\npm_0})+2\exp\quantity(-\frac{3\sqrt{y\vee L}n}{8}).

Note that the random variable
\delta_{t}-\E_{t-1}[\delta_{t}]
conditioned on round
t-1
satisfies
\quantity(\frac{2}{n},\frac{2(\alpha_{t-1}(i)+\alpha_{t-1}(j))}{n})
-Bernstein condition.
Then,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\exp\quantity(-\frac{3n}{8}\sqrt{y\vee L})dy

\displaystyle=L\exp\quantity(-\frac{3n}{8}\sqrt{L})+\int_{L}^{1}\exp\quantity(-\frac{3n}{8}\sqrt{y})dy

\displaystyle\leq L\exp\quantity(-\frac{3n}{8}\sqrt{L})+2\frac{(3/8)n\sqrt{L}+1}{(3/8)^{2}n^{2}}\exp\quantity(-\frac{3n}{8}\sqrt{L})

\displaystyle\leq\exp\quantity(-\Omega(\sqrt{n})).

Note that
\int\exp(-a\sqrt{x})dx=-\frac{2\exp(-a\sqrt{x})\quantity(a\sqrt{x}+1)}{a^{2}}
.
Furthermore, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\exp\quantity(-\frac{(y\vee L)n}{32(1+\cmaxup)\npm_0})dy

\displaystyle=L\exp\quantity(-\frac{Ln}{32(1+\cmaxup)\npm_0})+\int_{L}^{1}\exp\quantity(-\frac{yn}{32(1+\cmaxup)\npm_0})dy

\displaystyle\leq L\exp\quantity(-\frac{Ln}{32(1+\cmaxup)\npm_0})+\frac{32(1+c_{\max}^{\uparrow})\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}}{n}\exp\quantity(-\frac{Ln}{32(1+\cmaxup)\npm_0})

\displaystyle\leq 2L\exp\quantity(-\frac{Ln}{32(1+\cmaxup)\npm_0}).

Note that
\int\exp(-ax)dx=-\frac{\exp(-ax)}{a}
.
The last inequality follows from
\frac{\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}}{n}\leq\frac{16c^{2}}{nC_{\mbox{\tiny\ref{lem:taudeltaplus bounded jump}}}}
holds for sufficiently large
C_{\mbox{\tiny\ref{lem:taudeltaplus bounded jump}}}
.

Consequently, for
\tau>t-1
,

\displaystyle\E_{t-1}[\delta_{t}^{2}\mathbf{1}_{\delta_{t}^{2}>L}]

\displaystyle\leq R\quantity( \frac{n}{\constref{lem:taudeltaplus}\npm_0} \exp\qty(-\Omega(\sqrt{n}))
+ \frac{64c^2}{\constref{lem:taudeltaplus}\npm_0}\exp\qty(-\frac{c^2}{2(1+\cmaxup)\npm_0}) )

\displaystyle\leq R/2

holds since
\frac{c^{2}}{\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}}\geq\frac{C_{\mbox{\tiny\ref{lem:taudeltaplus bounded jump}}}}{16}
holds for sufficiently large
C_{\mbox{\tiny\ref{lem:taudeltaplus bounded jump}}}
.
∎

Lemma 5.14
.

Let
c_{1},c_{2}\in(0,1)
be arbitrary constants and let
x_{\delta}=c_{1}/\sqrt{n}
.
Suppose that
\psi_{0}\leq x_{\psi}
,
\beta_{0}\geq 1/2-x_{\beta}
,
\min\{\alpha_{0}(i),\alpha_{0}(j)\}\geq(1-c_{\mathrm{weak}})\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}
and
\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}\geq c_{2}
hold.
Then,

\displaystyle\Pr[\tau_{\delta}^{+}>1]\leq 1-c_{*}

for some
c_{*}\in(0,1)
depending only on
c_{1}
,
c_{2}
, and
C_{\mbox{\tiny\ref{lem:taudeltaplus}}}=\frac{(1-2c_{\mathrm{weak}})^{2}(1-c^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}})}{96(1-c_{\mathrm{weak}})}
.

Proof.

Since
n\delta_{1}=\sum_{v\in V}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathrm{opn}_{1}(v)=i}-\mathbf{1}_{\mathrm{opn}_{1}(v)=j})
is the sum of
n
independent random variables,

\lim_{n\to\infty}\Pr\frac{n\delta_{1}-\quantity[\frac{n\delta_1-\E[n\delta_1]}{\sqrt{\Var[n\delta_1]}}\leq x][n\delta_{1}]}{\sqrt{\Var[n\delta_{1}]}}\leq x=\Phi(x)
holds from the central limit theorem.
Here,
\Phi(x)=\int_{-\infty}^{x}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\exp\quantity(-\frac{y^2}{2})dy
is the cumulative distribution function of the standard normal distribution.
Noting
that

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{\delta_1}

\displaystyle\geq\delta_{1}-\absolutevalue{\delta_1-\E[\delta_1]}[\delta_{1}]-\absolutevalue{\E[\delta_1]}[\delta_{1}]=\delta_{1}-\absolutevalue{\delta_1-\E[\delta_1]}[\delta_{1}]-\absolutevalue{\delta_0(\alpha_1(i)+\alpha_1(j)+2(1-\beta_0))}\geq\delta_{1}-\absolutevalue{\delta_1-\E[\delta_1]}[\delta_{1}]-2x_{\delta}

and

\displaystyle\Var[\delta_{1}]\geq\frac{C_{\mbox{\tiny\ref{lem:taudeltaplus}}}\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}}{n}\geq\frac{C_{\mbox{\tiny\ref{lem:taudeltaplus}}}c_{2}}{n}

hold, we obtain

\displaystyle\Pr[\tau_{\delta}^{+}>1]

\displaystyle=\Pr\quantity[\taudeltaplus>1 \text{ and } \abs{\delta_1}<\xdelta]

\displaystyle\leq\Pr\quantity[\abs{\delta_1-\E[\delta_1]}<3\xdelta\text{ and } \taudeltaplus>1 ]<3x_{\delta}\text{ and }\tau_{\delta}^{+}>1

\displaystyle\leq\Pr\frac{\delta_{1}-\absolutevalue{\frac{\delta_1-\E[\delta_1]}{\sqrt{\Var[\delta_1]}}}[\delta_{1}]}{\sqrt{\quantity[\abs{\frac{\delta_1-\E[\delta_1]}{\sqrt{\Var[\delta_1]}}}<\frac{3\xdelta}{\sqrt{\Var[\delta_1]}}][\delta_{1}]}}<\frac{3x_{\delta}}{\sqrt{\Var[\delta_{1}]}}

\displaystyle\leq\Pr\frac{n\delta_{1}-\absolutevalue{\frac{n\delta_1-\E[n\delta_1]}{\sqrt{\Var[n\delta_1]}}}[n\delta_{1}]}{\sqrt{\quantity[\abs{\frac{n\delta_1-\E[n\delta_1]}{\sqrt{\Var[n\delta_1]}}}<3\sqrt{\frac{c_1^2}{\constref{lem:taudeltaplus}c_2}}][n\delta_{1}]}}<3\sqrt{\frac{c_{1}^{2}}{C_{\mbox{\tiny\ref{lem:taudeltaplus}}}c_{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq 1-2\Psi\quantity(-3\sqrt{\frac{c_1^2}{\constref{lem:taudeltaplus}c_2}})-o(1)

\displaystyle\leq 1-c_{*}.

∎

Proof of
Lemma

5.11
(
Item

2
).

Note that
(c_{\delta}^{+})^{2}=C_{\mbox{\tiny\ref{lem:hitting time for tnpm and npm}(\ref{item:taualphamaxup})}}\frac{C_{\mbox{\tiny\ref{lem:taudeltaplus}}}}{64}
from definition.
First, consider the case where
\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}\geq 16(c_{\delta}^{+})^{2}/C_{\mbox{\tiny\ref{lem:taudeltaplus bounded jump}}}
.
For convenience, let
\tau=\min\{\tau_{\delta}^{+},\tau_{\beta}^{-},\tau_{\psi}^{+},\tau^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}},\tau_{i}^{\mathrm{weak}},\tau_{j}^{\mathrm{weak}},\tau_{\max}^{\uparrow}\}
.
In this case, Combining
Lemmas

5.12
and
5.13
, we have

\displaystyle\E[\tau]\leq\frac{n}{C_{\mbox{\tiny\ref{lem:taudeltaplus}}}\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}}\frac{16(c_{\delta}^{+})^{2}}{n}+\frac{C_{\mbox{\tiny\ref{lem:taudeltaplus}}}\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}}{2n}\quantity(\frac{16(\cdeltaplus)^2}{n}+\frac{\constref{lem:taudeltaplus}\npm_0}{2n}\E[\tau])[\tau]=\frac{16(c_{\delta}^{+})^{2}}{C_{\mbox{\tiny\ref{lem:taudeltaplus}}}\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}}+\frac{\E[\tau]}{2},

i.e,
\E[\tau]\leq\frac{32(c_{\delta}^{+})^{2}}{C_{\mbox{\tiny\ref{lem:taudeltaplus}}}}\cdot\frac{1}{\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}}=\frac{C_{\mbox{\tiny\ref{lem:hitting time for tnpm and npm}(\ref{item:taualphamaxup})}}}{2\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}}
.
Thus, from the Markov inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\Pr[\tau>\frac{C_{\mbox{\tiny\ref{lem:hitting time for tnpm and npm}(\ref{item:taualphamaxup})}}}{2\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}}]\leq\frac{1}{2}.

(20)

We obtain

\displaystyle\Pr\quantity[\min\{\taudeltaplus,\tauiweak,\taujweak,\tautildemaxdown,\taupsiplus,\taubetaminus\}>T]

\displaystyle\leq\Pr\quantity[\tau>T]+\Pr\quantity[\taubetaminus>T \text{ and }\taumaxup\leq T]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}+n\exp(-\Omega(n(\npm_0)^2))

\displaystyle(\text{by \lx@cref{creftypeplural\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:taudeltaplus const prob 1} and\lx@nobreakspace\lx@cref{refnum}{lem:hitting time for tnpm and npm} (\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{item:taualphamaxup}}))

\displaystyle\leq 1-c_{*}.

Second, consider the case where
\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}\leq 16(c_{\delta}^{+})^{2}/C_{\mbox{\tiny\ref{lem:taudeltaplus bounded jump}}}
.
In this case, we have

\displaystyle\Pr[\tau_{\delta}^{+}>1]\leq 1-c_{*}

from
Lemma

5.14
.
Taking
C_{\mbox{\tiny\ref{lem:taudeltaplus bounded jump}}}\geq 16(c_{\delta}^{+})^{2}/C_{\mbox{\tiny\ref{lem:hitting time for tnpm and npm}(\ref{item:taualphamaxup})}}
,

\frac{C_{\mbox{\tiny\ref{lem:hitting time for tnpm and npm}(\ref{item:taualphamaxup})}}}{\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}}\geq C_{\mbox{\tiny\ref{lem:hitting time for tnpm and npm}(\ref{item:taualphamaxup})}}\frac{C_{\mbox{\tiny\ref{lem:taudeltaplus bounded jump}}}}{16(c_{\delta}^{+})^{2}}\geq 1
and we obtain the claim.

∎

Either of two non-weak opinions becomes weak: Proof of
Lemma

5.10
.

Now, we are ready to prove
Lemma

5.10
(
Items

1
and
2
).
To this end, we invoke the known drift analysis result of
[
17
]
.
Specifically, we use
Lemma

A.6
, which is a modified version from
[
31
]
.

Proof of
Lemma

5.10
(
Item

1
).

We apply
Lemma

A.6
for
Z_{t}=\mathrm{opn}_{t}
,
\varphi(Z_{t})=\sqrt{n}\cdot\absolutevalue{\delta_t(i,j)}
,

\tau=\min\quantity{\tauiweak,\taujweak,\tautildemaxdown,\taubetaminus,\taupsiplus},

c_{\varphi}^{\uparrow}=c_{\delta}^{\uparrow}
,
x_{0}=c_{\delta}^{+}
, and
x^{*}=\sqrt{C\log n}
.
Define

\displaystyle\tau^{\uparrow}=\begin{cases}\tau_{\delta}^{\uparrow}(i,j)&\text{if }\delta_{0}(i,j)\geq 0,\\
\tau_{\delta}^{\uparrow}(j,i)&\text{if }\delta_{0}(j,i)>0\;(\text{i.e., }\delta_{0}(i,j)<0)\end{cases},

where
\tau_{\delta}^{\uparrow}(i,j)=\tau_{\delta}^{\uparrow}=\inf\{t\geq 0:\delta_{t}(i,j)\geq(1+c_{\delta}^{\uparrow})\delta_{0}(i,j)\}
.
Then, from these settings, we have

\displaystyle\tau^{+}_{\varphi}(x_{0})=\inf\{t\geq 0:\sqrt{n}\absolutevalue{\delta_t}\geq c_{\delta}^{+}\}=\tau_{\delta}^{+},

\displaystyle\tau^{\uparrow}_{\varphi}=\inf\{t\geq 0:\sqrt{n}\absolutevalue{\delta_t}\geq(1+c_{\delta}^{\uparrow})\sqrt{n}\absolutevalue{\delta_0}\}\leq\tau^{\uparrow}.

From
Lemma

5.11
(
Item

2
), we have

\displaystyle\Pr\quantity[\min\qty{\tauphiplus(x_0),\tau} > \frac{\constrefs{lem:hitting time for tnpm and npm}{item:taualphamaxup}}{\npm_0}]\leq 1-c_{*}

for some constant
c_{*}>0
, i.e., the first condition of
Lemma

A.6
holds for
C_{1}=c_{*}
.
Note that if
\beta_{0}<\frac{1}{2}-x_{\beta}
or
\psi_{0}>x_{\psi}
or
\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}\leq O(\sqrt{\log n/n})
, then
\tau=0
.
Next, from
Lemma

5.11
(
Item

1
),

\displaystyle\Pr\quantity[\min\{\tauphiup,\tau\}> \frac{\constrefs{lem:hitting time for tnpm and npm}{item:taualphamaxup}}{\npm_0}]

\displaystyle\leq\Pr\quantity[\min\{\tau^\uparrow,\tau\}> \frac{\constrefs{lem:hitting time for tnpm and npm}{item:taualphamaxup}}{\npm_0}]

\displaystyle\leq\exp\quantity(-\Omega\qty(n\delta_0(i,j)^2))+n\exp\quantity(-\Omega\qty(n(\npm_0)^2))

\displaystyle\leq\exp\quantity(-\Omega\qty(\varphi(Z_0)^2))

holds for
\delta_{0}(i,j)\leq C\sqrt{\log n/n}
, i.e., the second condition of
Lemma

A.6
holds for some positive constant
C_{2}
.
Note that
n\exp\quantity(-\Omega\qty(n(\npm_0)^2))\leq n^{-\omega(1)}\leq\exp\quantity(-\Omega\qty(n\delta_0(i,j)^2))
from the assumption on
\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}=\omega(\sqrt{\log n/n})
.
Note that if
\beta_{0}<\frac{1}{2}-x_{\beta}
or
\psi_{0}>x_{\psi}
or
\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}\leq O(\sqrt{\log n/n})
, then
\tau=0
.

Write
\tau^{\mathrm{weak}}=\min\{\tau_{i}^{\mathrm{weak}},\tau_{j}^{\mathrm{weak}}\}
and
\tau_{ini}=\min\{\tau^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}},\tau_{\beta}^{-},\tau_{\psi}^{+}\}
.
From
Lemmas

A.6
and
5.8
with
\varepsilon=n^{-30}
, for some
T^{\prime}=O(\log n/\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0})
,

\displaystyle\Pr\quantity[\min\{\taudeltaplus(\sqrt{C\log n/n}),\tauweak\}> T' \text{ or } \tau_{ini}\leq T']

\displaystyle\leq\Pr\quantity[\qty{\min\{\tauphiplus(x_*),\tauweak\}> T' \text{ or } \tau_{ini}\leq T'}\text{ and } \tau_{ini}>T']+\Pr\quantity[\tau_{ini}\leq T']

\displaystyle\leq\Pr\quantity[\min\{\tauphiplus(x_*),\tauweak,\tau_{ini}\}> T']+\Pr\quantity[\tau_{ini}\leq T']

\displaystyle\leq n^{-20}.

∎

Proof of
Lemma

5.10
(
Item

2
).

Let
\tau^{\uparrow(s)}=\inf\{t\geq 0:\delta_{t}\geq(1+c)^{s}\delta_{0}\}
.
Let
\tau^{*}=\min\{\tau^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}},\tau_{\beta}^{-},\tau_{\psi}^{+}\}
.
From definition, for some
\ell=\Theta(\log n)
,
\tau_{j}^{\mathrm{weak}}\leq\tau^{\uparrow(\ell)}
holds.
Let
T^{\prime}=\frac{3C_{\mbox{\tiny\ref{lem:hitting time for tnpm and npm}(\ref{item:taualphamaxup})}}}{(1-c^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}})\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}}
.
Applying
Lemma

A.7
(
Item

3
), we have

\displaystyle\Pr\quantity[\min\{\taujweak,\tau^*\}>\ell T']

\displaystyle\leq\Pr\quantity[\min\{\tau^{\uparrow(\ell)},\tau^*\}>\ell T']

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s=1}^{\ell}\E\quantity[\indicator_{\tau^*>\tau^{\uparrow(s-1)}} \Pr_{\tau^{\uparrow(s-1)}}\qty[\min\{\tau^{\uparrow(s)},\tau^*\}> \tau^{\uparrow(s-1)}+\frac{3\constrefs{lem:hitting time for tnpm and npm}{item:taualphamaxup}}{(1-\ctildemaxdown)\npm_{0}}]]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s=1}^{\ell}\E\quantity[\indicator_{\tau^*>\tau^{\uparrow(s-1)}} \Pr_{\tau^{\uparrow(s-1)}}\qty[\min\{\tau^{\uparrow(s)},\tau^*\}> \tau^{\uparrow(s-1)}+\frac{\constrefs{lem:hitting time for tnpm and npm}{item:taualphamaxup}}{\npm_{\tau^{\uparrow(s-1)}}}]]

\displaystyle\leq\ell\quantity(\exp(-\Omega(n\delta_0^2))+n\exp\qty(-\Omega\qty(n (\npm_0)^2))).

Note that
\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{\tau^{\uparrow(s-1)}}\geq(1-c^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}})\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}/3\geq(1-c^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}})\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}/3\geq\omega(\sqrt{\log n/n})
holds.
∎

5.6
Emergence of Unique Strong Opinion

In this section, we show that once an opinion becomes weak, it cannot become
strong
—a key concept defined in
Definition

5.15
—for a sufficiently long time.
Recall
\eta_{t}(j)=\delta_{t}^{(c_{\eta})}(I_{t},j)=\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t}-(1+c_{\eta})\alpha_{t}(j)
and stopping times for
\eta_{t}(j)
as defined in
Definitions

4.1
and
4.16
.
We define the strong opinion and the stopping time for the emergence and persistence of the unique strong opinion,
\tau_{us}^{+}
and
\tau_{us}^{-}
:

Definition 5.15

(Strong Opinion)
.

We call that an opinion
j\in[k]
is
strong
at time
t
if
\alpha_{t}(j)\geq(1-c_{\mathrm{strong}})\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t}
.
By default, we set
c_{\mathrm{strong}}=0.05<c_{\mathrm{weak}}=0.1
.

Set
c_{\eta}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\frac{c_{\mathrm{strong}}}{1-c_{\mathrm{strong}}}
.
For a positive parameter
x
, define

\displaystyle\tau_{us}^{-}(x)\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\inf\quantity{t\geq 0: \min_{j\neq I_t}\eta_t(j)< x}\quad\text{and}\quad\tau_{us}^{+}(x)\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\inf\quantity{t\geq 0: \min_{j\neq I_t}\eta_t(j)\geq x}.

The fundamental relationships between the notions of weak, strong, and the value of
\eta_{t}(j)
are as follows:

1.

For any weak opinion
j
, we have
\eta_{t}(j)\geq\frac{c_{\mathrm{weak}}-c_{\mathrm{strong}}}{1-c_{\mathrm{strong}}}\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t}
.

2.

The opinion
j
is not strong if
\eta_{t}(j)>0
.

In summary, if an opinion
j
is weak, then
\eta_{t}(j)=\Omega(\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t})>0
(see
Item

1
), and if
\eta_{t}(j)>0
, then
j
is not a strong opinion (
Item

2
). Therefore, if
\min_{j\neq I_{t}}\eta_{t}(j)\geq x
(with
x>0
), it means that all opinions other than
I_{t}
are not strong; that is,
I_{t}
is the unique strong opinion at time
t
.
Note that
Item

1
follows from
\eta_{t}(j)=\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t}-\frac{1}{1-c_{\mathrm{strong}}}\alpha_{t}(j)\geq\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t}-\frac{1-c_{\mathrm{weak}}}{1-c_{\mathrm{strong}}}\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t}
, while
Item

2
follows from the inequality
\frac{\alpha_{t}(j)}{\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t}}<\frac{1}{1+c_{\eta}}=1-c_{\mathrm{strong}}
.

We show in this section that, with high probability,
(i) there exists exactly one strong opinion within
O(\log n/\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0})
rounds (
Item

1
of
Lemma

5.16
), and
(ii) any non-strong opinions keep non-strong for a sufficiently long time (
Item

2
of
Lemma

5.16
).

Lemma 5.16

(Unique Strong Opinion Lemma)
.

Suppose that
\psi_{0}\leq x_{\psi}
,
\beta_{0}\geq 1/2-x_{\beta}
, and
\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}=\omega(\log n/\sqrt{n})
.
Then, we have the following:

1.

For some
T=O(\log n/\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0})
, we have

\Pr\quantity[\tau_{us}^+(x_{\eta})> T \text{ or } \min\{\tautildemaxdown,\taubetaminus,\taupsiplus\}\leq T]\leq O(n^{-10}).

2.

Suppose
\eta_{0}(j)\geq x_{\eta}
for all
j\neq I_{0}
.
Let
C_{\mbox{\tiny\ref{lem:hitting time for tnpm and npm}(\ref{item:tildemaxdown})}}=c^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}}/36
be a positive constant that is defined in
Lemma

5.6
(
Item

1
).
Then, for any
T\leq C_{\mbox{\tiny\ref{lem:hitting time for tnpm and npm}(\ref{item:tildemaxdown})}}n
, we have

\Pr\quantity[\min\{\tau_{us}^-(x_{\eta}/2),\tautildemaxdown,\taubetaminus,\taupsiplus\}\leq T ]\leq n^{-10}.

The key lemma to prove
Lemma

5.16
is the following lemma, which shows that once
\eta_{t}(j)
becomes large, it stays large for a sufficiently long time—that is, once an opinion becomes weak, it cannot become strong for a long time.

Lemma 5.17

(Bounded decrease of
\eta_{t}(j)
)
.

Suppose that
\psi_{0}\leq x_{\psi}
,
\beta_{0}\geq 1/2-x_{\beta}
,
\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}=\omega(\log n/\sqrt{n})
, and
\eta_{0}(j)\geq x_{\eta}
.
Then, for any
T\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\Pr\quantity[\tauetaminus(x_{\eta}/2) \leq T \text{ and } \min\{\tautildemaxdown,\taubetaminus,\taupsiplus\}>T]\leq Tn^{-\omega(1)}.

Weak cannot be strong: Proof of
Lemma

5.17
.

To prove
Lemma

5.17
, we first prove the following lemma:

Lemma 5.18
.

Let
y=y(n)
and
I=I(n)
be positive parameters satisfying the following:

I=\omega(x_{\beta})
,
y=\omega(I)
, and
y=o(\log n/\sqrt{n})
. Suppose that
\beta_{0}\geq 1/2-x_{\beta}
,
\psi_{0}\leq x_{\psi}
,
\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}\geq\omega\quantity(\log n/\sqrt{n})
, and
\eta_{0}(j)\in[y,y+I]
.
Then, for any
T\geq\frac{24(1+c_{\eta})}{1-c^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}}}\cdot\frac{I}{(y-I)\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}}
, we have

\displaystyle\Pr\quantity[\tauetaminus(y-I)<\tauetaplus(y+2I) \text{ and } \min\{\tautildemaxdown,\taumaxup,\taubetaminus,\taupsiplus\}>T]\leq n^{-\omega(1)}.

Proof.

Let
\tau^{*}=\min\{\tau^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}},\tau_{\max}^{\uparrow},\tau_{\beta}^{-},\tau_{\psi}^{+}\}
and
let
\tau=\min\{\tau^{+}_{\eta}(y+2I),\tau^{-}_{\eta}(y-I),\tau^{*}\}
.
For
t-1<\tau
, we have

\displaystyle\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}+\alpha_{t-1}(j)+1-2\beta_{t-1}

\displaystyle=\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}\quantity(1+\frac{\npm_{t-1}-\eta_{t-1}(j)}{(1+\ceta)\npm_{t-1}}-\frac{\gamma_{t-1}}{\beta_{t-1}\npm_{t-1}}-\frac{\psi_{t-1}}{\beta_{t-1}^2\tnpm_{t-1}})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{3}(1-c^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}})\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}\quantity(\frac{1}{1+\ceta}-\underbrace{\frac{y+2I}{(1+\ceta)(1/3)(1-\ctildemaxdown)\tnpm_{0}}}_{o(1)}-\underbrace{\frac{\xpsi}{(1/3)^2(1-\ctildemaxdown)\tnpm_{0}}}_{o(1) })

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1-c^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}}}{6(1+c_{\eta})}\cdot\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}.

(21)

Hence, for
t-1<\tau
, we have

\displaystyle\E_{t-1}[\eta_{t}(j)]

\displaystyle\geq\E_{t-1}[\delta_{t}^{(c_{\eta})}(I_{t-1},j)]

\displaystyle\geq\delta_{t-1}^{(c_{\eta})}(I_{t-1},j)+\delta_{t-1}^{(c_{\eta})}(I_{t-1},j)\quantity(\alpha_{t-1}(I_{t-1})+\alpha_{t-1}(j)+1-2\beta_{t-1})

\displaystyle\geq\eta_{t-1}(j)+\frac{1-c^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}}}{6(1+c_{\eta})}\cdot(y-I)\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}.

Note that we use
Lemma

4.4
(
Item

1
).

Letting
X_{t}=\eta_{t}(j)
and
R=\frac{1-c^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}}}{6(1+c_{\eta})}\cdot(y-I)\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}>0
, we have

\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}X_{t-1}+R-\quantity(X_{t-1}+\drift-\E_{t-1}[X_t])_{t-1}[X_{t}]=\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}\eta_{t-1}(j)+R-\quantity(\eta_{t-1}(j)+\drift-\E_{t-1}[\eta_t(j)])_{t-1}[\eta_{t}(j)]\leq 0

and

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}\quantity(X_{t-1}+\drift-X_t)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}\quantity(\eta_{t-1}(j)+\drift-\eta_t(j))

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}\quantity(\E_{t-1}[\delta_{t}^{(\ceta)}(I_{t-1},j)]-\delta_{t}^{(\ceta)}(I_{t-1},j))_{t-1}[\delta_{t}^{(c_{\eta})}(I_{t-1},j)]-\delta_{t}^{(c_{\eta})}(I_{t-1},j),

i.e.,
\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}\quantity(X_{t-1}+\drift-X_t)
satisfies one-sided
\quantity(O(1/n),O(\npm_0/n))
-Bernstein condition.
Note that we use
Lemma

4.11
(
Item

3
) and
Lemma

3.7
(
Items

2
and
3
).

Let
I^{-}=y-I
,
I^{-}_{*}=y
,
I^{+}_{*}=y+I
, and
I^{+}=y+2I
.
Note that
I^{+}-I^{+}_{*}=I^{+}_{*}-I^{-}_{*}=I^{-}_{*}-I^{-}=I
.
Let
T\geq\frac{24(1+c_{\eta})}{1-c^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}}}\frac{I}{(y-I)\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}}\geq\frac{2(I^{+}-X_{0})}{R}
.
Applying
Lemma

3.11
(
Item

2
) for
\eta_{0}(j)\in[I_{*}^{-},I^{+}_{*}]
, we have

\displaystyle\Pr\quantity[\tauetaminus(y-I)<\tauetaplus(y+2I) \text{ and } \tau^*>T]\leq\exp\quantity(-\Omega\qty(\frac{I^2}{\frac{\npm_0}{n}T+\frac{I}{n}}))\leq\exp\quantity(-\Omega\qty(nI(y-I))).

∎

Next, we prove the following lemma assuring
\eta_{t}(j)
does not decrease too much in one step.

Lemma 5.19
.

Let
I=I(n)
be a positive parameter satisfying
I=\omega(x_{\psi})
and

\tau^{jump}_{\eta}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\inf\{t\geq 0:\eta_{t}(j)\leq\eta_{t-1}(j)-I\}
.
Suppose that
\beta_{0}\geq 1/2-x_{\beta}
and
\psi_{0}\leq x_{\psi}
.
Then, for any
T\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\Pr\quantity[\tau^{jump}_\eta\leq T \text{ and } \min\{\taubetaminus, \taupsiplus\}>T]\leq Tn^{-\omega(1)}.

Proof.

Let
\tau=\min\{\tau_{\beta}^{-},\tau_{\psi}^{+}\}
.
For
t-1<\tau
, we have
2\beta_{t-1}-1\leq\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}+3x_{\psi}
.
Hence, for
t-1<\tau
, we have

\displaystyle\E_{t-1}[\eta_{t}(j)]

\displaystyle\geq\E_{t-1}\quantity[\delta_t^{(\ceta)}(I_{t-1},j)]

\displaystyle\geq\eta_{t-1}(j)\quantity(1+\npm_{t-1}+\alpha_{t-1}(j)+1-2\beta_{t-1})

\displaystyle\geq\eta_{t-1}(j)\quantity(1-3\xpsi)

\displaystyle\geq\eta_{t-1}(j)-3x_{\psi}.

Thus,

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}\quantity(\eta_{t-1}(j)-3\xpsi-\eta_t(j))

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}\quantity(\E_{t-1}\qty[\delta_t^{(\ceta)}(I_{t-1},j)]-\delta_t^{(\ceta)}(I_{t-1},j))_{t-1}\quantity[\delta_t^{(\ceta)}(I_{t-1},j)]-\delta_{t}^{(c_{\eta})}(I_{t-1},j)

holds.
From
Lemma

3.7
(
Items

3
and
2
) and
Lemma

4.4
(
Item

3
), we have that the random variable
\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}\quantity(\E_{t-1}\qty[\delta_t^{(\ceta)}(I_{t-1},j)]- \delta_t^{(\ceta)}(I_{t-1},j))_{t-1}\quantity[\delta_t^{(\ceta)}(I_{t-1},j)]-\delta_{t}^{(c_{\eta})}(I_{t-1},j)
conditioned on round
t-1
satisfies
\quantity(\frac{4}{n},\frac{10}{n})
-Bernstein condition.
From
Item

4
of
Lemma

3.7
,

\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}\quantity(\eta_{t-1}(j)-3\xpsi-\eta_t(j))
satisfies one-sided
\quantity(\frac{4}{n},\frac{10}{n})
-Bernstein condition.
Thus,

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}\Pr_{t-1}\quantity[\eta_t(j)\leq\eta_{t-1}(j)-I]

\displaystyle=\E_{t-1}\quantity[\indicator_{\tau>t-1}\indicator_{\eta_{t-1}(j)-3\xpsi-\eta_t(j)\geq I-3\xpsi}]

\displaystyle=\Pr_{t-1}\quantity[\tau>t-1 \text{ and } \eta_{t-1}(j)-3\xpsi-\eta_t(j)\geq I-3\xpsi]

\displaystyle=\Pr_{t-1}\quantity[\indicator_{\tau>t-1}\qty(\eta_{t-1}(j)-3\xpsi-\eta_t(j))\geq I-3\xpsi]

\displaystyle\leq\exp\quantity(-\Omega\qty(nI^2))

\displaystyle\leq n^{-\omega(1)}.

Thus, we obtain

\displaystyle\Pr\quantity[\tau^{jump}_\eta\leq T \text{ and } \tau>T]

\displaystyle=\Pr\quantity[\exists t\leq T: \eta_t(j)\leq\eta_{t-1}(j)-I\text{ and }\tau>T]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\E\quantity[\indicator_{\tau>t-1}\Pr_{t-1}\qty[\eta_t(j)\leq\eta_{t-1}(j)-I]]

\displaystyle\leq Tn^{-\omega(1)}.

∎

Proof of
Lemma

5.17
.

Let
y=y(n)
and
I=I(n)
be positive parameters satisfying the following:

I=\omega(x_{\beta})
,
y=\omega(I)
, and
y=o(\log n/\sqrt{n})
. Let
I^{-}=y-I
,
I^{-}_{*}=y
,
I^{+}_{*}=y+I
, and
I^{+}=y+2I
.
Note that
I^{+}-I^{+}_{*}=I^{+}_{*}-I^{-}_{*}=I^{-}_{*}-I^{-}=I
.
Let
T^{\prime}=\frac{24(1+c_{\eta})}{1-c^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}}}\frac{I}{(y-I)\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}}=o\quantity(\frac{1}{\npm_0})
.
Then, combining

Lemmas

5.18
and
5.6
,
Lemma

5.2
,
if
\beta_{0}\geq 1/2-x_{\beta}
,
\psi_{0}\leq x_{\psi}
,
\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}=\omega(\log n/\sqrt{n})
, and
\eta_{0}(j)\in[I^{-}_{*},I^{+}_{*}]
,

\displaystyle\Pr\quantity[\tauetaminus(y-I)<\tauetaplus(y+2I)]

\displaystyle\leq\Pr\quantity[\tauetaminus(y-I)<\tauetaplus(y+2I) \text{ and } \min\{\tautildemaxdown,\taumaxup,\taubetaminus,\taupsiplus\}>T']

\displaystyle+\Pr\quantity[\tautildemaxdown\leq T'\text{ and } \min\{\taumaxup,\taubetaminus,\taupsiplus\}>T']+\Pr\quantity[\taumaxup\leq T'\text{ and } \min\{\taubetaminus,\taupsiplus\}>T']

\displaystyle+\Pr\quantity[\taubetaminus\leq T'\text{ and } \taupsiplus>T']+\Pr\quantity[\taupsiplus\leq T']

\displaystyle\leq n^{-\omega(1)}.

Let
\tau^{*}=\min\{\tau^{jump}_{\eta},\tau^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}},\tau_{\beta}^{-},\tau_{\psi}^{+}\}
.
Let
\tau_{s}^{-}=\inf\{t\geq s:\eta_{t}(j)\leq I^{-}\}
and
\tau_{s}^{+}=\inf\{t\geq s:\eta_{t}(j)\geq I^{+}\}
.
Suppose
\eta_{0}(j)\in[I^{-}_{*},I^{+}_{*}]
,
\beta_{0}\geq 1/2-x
,
\psi_{0}\leq x
, and
\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}\geq\omega(\log n/\sqrt{n})
.
Applying
Lemma

A.7
(
Item

2
), we have

\displaystyle\Pr\quantity[\tauetaminus(y-I) \leq T \text{ and } \tau^*>T]\leq\sum_{s=0}^{T-1}\E\quantity[\indicator_{\eta_s(j)\in[I^-_*,I^{+}_*] \text{ and } \tau^*>s}\Pr_s\qty[\tau^-_s<\tau^+_s]]\leq Tn^{-\omega(1)}.

∎

Emergence of unique strong opinion: Proof of
Lemma

5.16
.

Proof of
Lemma

5.16
(
Item

1
).

Fix an arbitrary pair of distinct opinions
i
and
j
.
Observe the following holds:

•

Combining
Items

2
and
1
of
Lemma

5.10
,
for some
T_{1}=O(\log n/\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0})
,
\min\{\alpha_{T_{1}}(i),\alpha_{T_{1}}(j)\}\leq(1-c_{\mathrm{weak}})\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{T_{1}}
,
\beta_{T_{1}}\geq 1/2-x_{\beta}
,
\psi_{T_{1}}\leq x_{\psi}
, and
\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{T_{1}}=\Omega(\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0})=\omega(\log n/\sqrt{n})
with probability at least
1-n^{-10}
.
Suppose that
\alpha_{T_{1}}(j)\leq(1-c_{\mathrm{weak}})\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{T_{1}}
without loss of generality.
From the definition of
\eta_{t}(j)
, we have
\eta_{T_{1}}(j)\geq\frac{c_{\mathrm{weak}}-c_{\mathrm{strong}}}{1-c_{\mathrm{strong}}}\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{T_{1}}=\Omega(\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0})\geq 2x_{\eta}
.

•

From
Lemma

5.17
, for any
t\in[T_{1},T_{1}+T_{2}]
, where
T_{2}\geq n\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{T_{1}}/C\log n=\Omega(n\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}/\log n)
,
\eta_{t}(j)\geq x_{\eta}
,
\beta_{t}\geq 1/2-x_{\beta}
,
\psi_{t}\leq x_{\psi}
, and
\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t}=\Omega(\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0})=\omega(\log n/\sqrt{n})
with probability at least
1-n^{-\omega(1)}
.

Since
T_{1}=O(\log n/\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0})=o(\sqrt{n})
and
T_{2}=\Omega(n\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}/\log n)=\omega(\sqrt{n})
, we have
T_{1}<T_{2}
.
Thus, by taking the union bound over all pairs of distinct opinions
i,j\in[k]
, with high probability, there exists
t\leq T_{1}
such that
\eta_{t}(j)\geq x_{\eta}
for all
j\neq I_{t}
.
∎

Proof of
Lemma

5.16
(
Item

2
).

Consider an arbitrary opinion
j\neq I_{0}
. Combining
Lemmas

5.17
and
5.8
,

\displaystyle\Pr\quantity[\tauetaminus(x_{\eta}/2) \leq T \text{ or } \min\{\tautildemaxdown,\taubetaminus,\taupsiplus\}\leq T]

\displaystyle\leq\Pr\quantity[\qty{\tauetaminus(x_{\eta}/2) \leq T \text{ or } \min\{\tautildemaxdown,\taubetaminus,\taupsiplus\}\leq T} \text{ and } \min\{\tautildemaxdown,\taubetaminus,\taupsiplus\}>T]

\displaystyle+\Pr\quantity[\min\{\tautildemaxdown,\taubetaminus,\taupsiplus\}\leq T]

\displaystyle\leq n^{-11}.

(22)

Thus, from the union bound,

\displaystyle\Pr\quantity[\min\{\tau_{us}^-(x_{\eta}/2),\tautildemaxdown,\taubetaminus,\taupsiplus\}\leq T ]\leq\sum_{j\neq I_{0}}\Pr\quantity[\min\{\tau_\eta^-(x_{\eta}/2),\tautildemaxdown,\taubetaminus,\taupsiplus\}\leq T]\leq n^{-10}.

∎

5.7
Towards Consensus

We now turn to establishing an upper bound on the consensus time.
Specifically, we show that once there remains exactly one strong opinion, the process will reach consensus within
O(\log n/\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0})
rounds with high probability (see
Lemma

5.20
).

Lemma 5.20

(Unique strong opinion leads to consensus)
.

Suppose
\psi_{0}\leq x_{\psi}
,
\beta_{0}\geq 1/2-x_{\beta}
,
\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}=\omega(\log n/\sqrt{n})
, and
\eta_{0}(j)\geq x_{\eta}
for all
j\neq I_{0}
.
Then,
\Pr\quantity[\taucons>T]\leq 1/n
holds for some
T=O\quantity(\log n/\npm_0)
.

Define the following stopping times:

\displaystyle\tau_{\max}^{+}(x)\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\inf\{t\geq 0:\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{t}\geq x\},

\displaystyle\tau^{-}_{\max}(x)\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\inf\{t\geq 0:\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{t}\leq x\},

\displaystyle\tau^{+}_{\widetilde{\max}}(x)\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\inf\{t\geq 0:\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{t}\geq x\},

\displaystyle\tau_{\textrm{all}}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\inf\{t\geq 0:\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t}=\beta_{t}\}.

For simplicity, we sometimes assume that
\alpha_{0}(1)=\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}
.
The following lemma describes how the unique strong opinion evolves, which in turn implies
Lemma

5.20
.

Lemma 5.21
.

We have the following:

1.

Let
c\in(0,1)
be an arbitrary constant.
Suppose
\psi_{0}\leq x_{\psi}
,
\beta_{0}\geq 1/2-x_{\beta}
,
\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}=\omega(\log n/\sqrt{n})
, and
\eta_{0}(j)\geq x_{\eta}
for all
j\neq I_{0}
.
Then, for some
T=O\quantity(\log n/\npm_0)
,

\displaystyle\Pr\quantity[\tautildemaxplus(1-c)>T \text{ or } \min\{\taupsiplus,\taubetaminus\}\leq T]\leq n^{-10}.

2.

Suppose that
\psi_{0}\leq x_{\psi}
,
\beta_{0}\geq 1/2-x_{\beta}
, and
\alpha_{0}(1)\geq(1-c^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}})\beta_{0}
.
Then, for some
T=O(\log n)
,

\displaystyle\Pr\quantity[\taumaxplus(1-4\ctildemaxdown)>T \text{ or } \min\{\taupsi,\taubetaminus\}\leq T]\leq n^{-10}.

3.

Suppose that
\alpha_{0}(1)\geq 7/8
.
Then,

\Pr\quantity[\tau_{all}> 8\log n \text{ or } \taumaxminus(3/4)\leq 8\log n]\leq 1/n.

4.

Suppose
\alpha_{0}(1)=\beta_{0}
and
\beta_{0}\geq 1/2-x_{\beta}
.
Then,
\Pr[\tau_{\mathrm{cons}}>6\log n]\leq 1/n
.

Proof of
Lemma

5.21
(
Item

1
).

Let
\tau^{\uparrow}_{\widetilde{\max}}=\inf\{t\geq 0:\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{t}\geq(1+c^{\uparrow}_{\widetilde{\max}})\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}\}
for some positive constant
c^{\uparrow}_{\widetilde{\max}}\in(0,1)
.
Let
\tau^{*}=\min\{\tau^{+}_{\widetilde{\max}},\tau_{\beta}^{-},\tau_{us}^{-}(x_{\eta})\}
and
\tau=\min\{\tau^{\uparrow}_{\widetilde{\max}},\tau^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}},\tau^{*}\}
.
For
t-1<\tau
, we have

\displaystyle\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}-\frac{\gamma_{t-1}}{\beta_{t-1}}

\displaystyle\geq\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}-\frac{(\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1})^{2}}{\beta_{t-1}}-\frac{(1-c_{\mathrm{strong}})\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}\quantity(\beta_{t-1}-\npm_{t-1})}{\beta_{t-1}}

\displaystyle=c_{\mathrm{strong}}\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}\quantity(1-\tnpm_{t-1})

\displaystyle\geq c_{\mathrm{strong}}c\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}.

(23)

Hence, for
t-1<\tau
, we have

\displaystyle\E_{t-1}\quantity[\tnpm_t]

\displaystyle\geq\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}\quantity(1+\frac{\npm_{t-1}-\gamma_{t-1}/\beta_{t-1}}{2})-\frac{9\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}}{n}

\displaystyle\geq\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}\quantity(1+\frac{\cstrong c\npm_{t-1}}{2})-\frac{9\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}}{n}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}+\frac{c_{\mathrm{strong}}c\beta_{t-1}(\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1})^{2}}{2}-\frac{9\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}}{n}

\displaystyle\geq\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}+\frac{c_{\mathrm{strong}}c(1-c^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}})^{2}(\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0})^{2}}{12}.

Note that we use
Lemma

4.7
(
Item

1
)
and
\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}\geq\frac{(1-c^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}})\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}}{3}=\omega(1/n)
.

By letting
X_{t}=\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{t}
and
R=\frac{c_{\mathrm{strong}}c(1-c^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}})^{2}(\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0})^{2}}{12}>0
, we have

\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}X_{t-1}+R-\quantity(X_{t-1}+\drift-\E_{t-1}[X_t])_{t-1}[X_{t}]=\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}+R-\quantity(\tnpm_{t-1}+\drift-\E_{t-1}[\tnpm_t])_{t-1}[\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{t}]\leq 0.

Moreover, it holds that the random variable

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}\quantity(X_{t-1}+\drift-X_t)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}\quantity(\tnpm_{t-1}\qty(1+\frac{\npm_{t-1}-\gamma_{t-1}/\beta_{t-1}}{2})-\frac{9\npm_{t-1}}{n}-\tnpm_t)

satisfies one-sided
\quantity(O\qty(\frac{1}{n}),O\qty(\frac{\npm_0}{n}))
-Bernstein condition.
Note that we use
Lemma

4.7
(
Item

2
) and
Lemma

3.7
(
Items

2
and
3
).

Applying
Lemma

3.11
(
Item

2
) for
I^{+}=(1+c^{\uparrow}_{\widetilde{\max}})\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}
,
I^{-}=(1+c^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}})\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}
, and for
T^{\prime}=\frac{2(I^{+}-X_{0})}{R}=\frac{24}{c_{\mathrm{strong}}c^{+}(1-c^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}})^{2}}\frac{1}{\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}}
, we have

\displaystyle\Pr\quantity[\tautildemaxup> T' \text{ and } \tau^* > T']\leq\exp\quantity(- \Omega\qty( n (\npm_0)^2)).

Let
\tau^{\uparrow(s)}=\inf\{t\geq 0:\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{t}\geq(1+c^{\uparrow}_{\widetilde{\max}})^{s}\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}\}
.
From definition, for some
\ell=\Theta(\log n)
,
\tau^{+}_{\widetilde{\max}}\leq\tau^{\uparrow(\ell)}
holds.
Applying
Lemma

A.7
(
Item

3
), we obtain

\displaystyle\Pr\quantity[\min\{\tautildemaxplus,\tau^*\}>\ell T']

\displaystyle\leq\Pr\quantity[\min\{\tau^{\uparrow(\ell)},\tau^*\}>\ell T']

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s=1}^{\ell}\E\quantity[\indicator_{\tau^*>\tau^{\uparrow(s-1)}} \Pr_{\tau^{\uparrow(s-1)}}\qty[\min\{\tau^{\uparrow(s)},\tau^*\}> \tau^{\uparrow(s-1)}+T']]

\displaystyle\leq\ell\exp\quantity(-\Omega\qty(n (\npm_0)^2)).

Note that
\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{\tau^{\uparrow(s-1)}}\geq\Omega(\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0})
holds.

Finally, combining the above and
Eq.

22
, we have

\displaystyle\Pr\quantity[\tautildemaxplus>T \text{ or }\min\{\taupsi,\taubetaminus\}\leq T]

\displaystyle\leq\Pr\quantity[\qty{\tautildemaxplus>T \text{ or } \min\{\taupsi,\taubetaminus\}\leq T} \text{ and } \min\{\taupsi,\taubetaminus,\tau_{us}^-(x_{\eta}/2)\}>T]

\displaystyle+\Pr\quantity[\min\{\taupsi, \taubetaminus,\tau_{us}^-(x_{\eta}/2)\}\leq T]

\displaystyle\leq n^{-10}.

∎

Proof of
Lemma

5.21
(
Item

2
).

Let
\tau_{\max}^{\downarrow}=\inf\{t\geq 0:\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t}\leq(1-c_{\max}^{\downarrow})\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}\}
.
Let
\tau^{*}=\min\{\tau_{\max}^{+}(1-4c^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}}),\tau^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}},\tau_{\beta}^{-}\}
and
\tau=\min\{\tau_{\max}^{\uparrow},\tau_{\max}^{\downarrow},\tau^{*}\}
.
For
t-1<\tau
, we have

\displaystyle 1-\alpha_{t-1}(1)\quantity(\frac{2}{\tnpm_{t-1}}-1)

\displaystyle\geq 1-(1-4c^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}})\quantity(\frac{2}{(1-\ctildemaxdown)^2}-1)\geq\frac{8(c^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}})^{2}}{1-2c^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}}}.

Note that
\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}\geq(1-c^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}})\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}\geq(1-c^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}})^{2}\geq 1-2c^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}}
holds.
Hence, for any
t-1<\tau
, we have

\displaystyle\E_{t-1}[\alpha_{t}(1)]-\alpha_{t-1}(1)

\displaystyle=\alpha_{t-1}(1)\quantity(1-\alpha_{t-1}(1)\qty(\frac{2}{\tnpm_{t-1}}-1))\geq\frac{8(c^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}})^{2}(1-c_{\max}^{\downarrow})}{1-2c^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}}}\alpha_{0}(1).

By setting
X_{t}=\alpha_{t}(1)
and
R=\frac{8(c^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}})^{2}(1-c_{\max}^{\downarrow})}{1-2c^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}}}\alpha_{0}(1)>0
, we have

\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}X_{t-1}+R-\quantity(X_{t-1}+\drift-\E_{t-1}[X_t])_{t-1}[X_{t}]=\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}\alpha_{t-1}(1)+R-\quantity(\alpha_{t-1}(1)+\drift-\E_{t-1}[\alpha_t(1)])_{t-1}[\alpha_{t}(1)]\leq 0
.
Moreover, it holds that the random variable

\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}\quantity(\E_{t-1}[X_t]-X_t)_{t-1}[X_{t}]-X_{t}=\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}\quantity(\E_{t-1}[\alpha_t(1)]-\alpha_t(1))_{t-1}[\alpha_{t}(1)]-\alpha_{t}(1)\\

satisfies one-sided
\quantity(O\qty(\frac{1}{n}),O\qty(\frac{\alpha_{0}(1)}{n}))
-Bernstein condition.
Note that we use
Lemma

4.2
(
Item

3
) and
Lemma

3.7
(
Items

2
and
3
).

Applying
Lemma

3.11
(
Item

2
) for
I^{+}=(1+c_{\max}^{\uparrow})\alpha_{0}(1)
,
I^{-}=(1-c_{\max}^{\downarrow})\alpha_{0}(1)
, and

T^{\prime}=\frac{c_{\max}^{\uparrow}(1-2c^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}})}{4(c^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}})^{2}(1-c_{\max}^{\downarrow})}=\frac{2(I^{+}-X_{0})}{R}
, we have

\displaystyle\Pr\quantity[\taumaxup> T' \text{ and } \tau^* > T']\leq\exp\quantity(- \Omega\qty( n (\npm_0)^2)).

Let
\tau^{\uparrow(s)}=\inf\{t\geq 0:\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t}\geq(1+c_{\max}^{\uparrow})^{s}\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}\}
.
From definition, for some
\ell=\Theta(\log n)
,
\tau_{\max}^{+}(1-4c^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}})\leq\tau^{\uparrow(\ell)}
holds.
Applying
Lemma

A.7
(
Item

3
), we have

\displaystyle\Pr\quantity[\min\{\taumaxplus(1-4\ctildemaxdown),\tau^*\}>\ell T']

\displaystyle\leq\Pr\quantity[\min\{\tau^{\uparrow(\ell)},\tau^*\}>\ell T']

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s=1}^{\ell}\E\quantity[\indicator_{\tau^*>\tau^{\uparrow(s-1)}} \Pr_{\tau^{\uparrow(s-1)}}\qty[\min\{\tau^{\uparrow(s)},\tau^*\}> \tau^{\uparrow(s-1)}+T']]

\displaystyle\leq\ell\exp\quantity(-\Omega\qty(n (\npm_0)^2)).

Note that
\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{\tau^{\uparrow(s-1)}}\geq\Omega(\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0})
holds.

Finally, combining the above and
Lemma

5.8
, we have

\displaystyle\Pr\quantity[\taumaxplus(1-4\ctildemaxdown)>T \text{ or } \min\{\taupsi,\taubetaminus\}\leq T]

\displaystyle\leq\Pr\quantity[\qty{\taumaxplus(1-4\ctildemaxdown)>T \text{ or } \min\{\taupsi,\taubetaminus\}\leq T} \text{ and } \min\{\taupsi,\taubetaminus,\tautildemaxdown\}>T]

\displaystyle+\Pr\quantity[\min\{\taupsi, \taubetaminus,\tautildemaxdown\}\leq T]

\displaystyle\leq n^{-10}.

∎

Proof of
Lemma

5.21
(
Item

3
).

To begin with, we prove the following claim.

Claim 5.22
.

Let
c\in(0,1/2)
be an arbitrary constant.
Suppose that
\psi_{0}\leq x_{\psi}
,
\beta_{0}\geq 1/2-x_{\beta}
, and
\alpha_{0}(1)\geq 1-c
.
Then, for some
T=\Omega(1/x_{\psi})
,

\displaystyle\Pr\quantity[\taumaxminus(1-2c) \leq\min\{T,\taubetaminus,\taupsiplus\}]\leq\exp\quantity(-\Omega\qty(n)).

Proof.

Let
\tau=\min\{\tau_{\beta}^{-},\tau_{\psi}^{+}\}
.
Then, for
t-1<\tau
, we have

\displaystyle\E_{t-1}[\alpha_{t}(1)]

\displaystyle=\alpha_{t-1}(1)\quantity(1+\alpha_{t-1}(1)-\frac{\gamma_{t-1}+\xpsi}{\beta_{t-1}})\geq\alpha_{t-1}(1)-3x_{\psi}.

Note that we use
Lemma

4.2
(
Item

1
) in the first equality.

Hence, letting
X_{t}=\alpha_{t}(1)
and
R=-3x_{\psi}<0
, we have

\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}X_{t-1}+R-\quantity(X_{t-1}+\drift-\E_{t-1}[X_t])_{t-1}[X_{t}]=\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}\alpha_{t-1}(1)-3x_{\psi}-\quantity(\alpha_{t-1}(1)-3\xpsi-\E_{t-1}[\alpha_t(1)])_{t-1}[\alpha_{t}(1)]\leq 0

and
\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}\quantity(\E_{t-1}[X_t]-X_t)_{t-1}[X_{t}]-X_{t}=\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}\quantity(\E_{t-1}[\alpha_t(1)]-\alpha_t(1))_{t-1}[\alpha_{t}(1)]-\alpha_{t}(1)
satisfies
\quantity(O(1/n),O(1/n))
-Bernstein condition.
Note that we use
Lemma

4.2
(
Item

3
) and
Lemma

3.7
(
Items

2
and
3
).

Hence, applying
Lemma

3.11
(
Item

1
) with
I^{-}=1-2c
, for
T\leq\frac{c}{6x_{\psi}}\leq\frac{X_{0}-I^{-}}{-2R}
, we have

\displaystyle\Pr\quantity[\taumaxminus(1-2c) \leq\min\{T,\taubetaminus,\taupsiplus\}]\leq\exp\quantity(-\Omega\qty(n)).

(24)

∎

Write
g_{t}=\beta_{t}-\alpha_{t}(1)=\sum_{j\geq 2}\alpha_{t}(j)
for convenience.
Let
\tau=\min\{\tau_{all},\tau^{-}_{\max}(3/4)\}
.
Then, for
t-1<\tau
, we have

\displaystyle\E_{t-1}[g_{t}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\geq 2}\alpha_{t-1}(j)\quantity(1+\alpha_{t-1}(j)+1-2\beta_{t-1})\leq g_{t-1}\quantity(1-\frac{1}{4}).

Note that
\alpha_{t-1}(j)\leq 1-\alpha_{t-1}(1)\leq 1/4
and
\beta_{t-1}\geq\alpha_{t-1}(1)\geq 3/4
.

Let
r=1-\frac{1}{4}=\frac{3}{4}
,
X_{t}=r^{-t}g_{t}
, and
Y_{t}=X_{t\wedge\tau}
.
Then,

\displaystyle\E_{t-1}[Y_{t}]-Y_{t-1}=\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}\quantity(\E_{t-1}[X_t]-X_{t-1})_{t-1}[X_{t}]-X_{t-1}\leq\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}r^{-t}\quantity(r^{-t}\E_{t-1}[g_t]-r^{-(t-1)}g_{t-1})_{t-1}[g_{t}]-r^{-(t-1)}g_{t-1}\leq 0,

i.e.,
Y_{t}
is a submartingale.
Hence, we have
\E[Y_{T}]\leq\E[Y_{0}]=g_{0}\leq 1
and

\displaystyle\E[Y_{T}]\geq\E[X_{T}\mid\tau>T]\Pr[\tau>T]=r^{-T}\E[g_{T}\mid\tau>T]\Pr[\tau>T]\geq r^{-T}n^{-1}\Pr[\tau>T].

Consequently, we have

\displaystyle\Pr[\tau>T]\leq nr^{T}\leq n\exp\quantity(-\frac{T}{4})\leq 1/n^{2}.

Thus, combining the above,
5.22
,
5.2
and
5.3
gives

\displaystyle\Pr\quantity[\tau_{all}> T \text{ or } \taumaxminus(3/4)\leq T]

\displaystyle\leq\Pr\quantity[\qty{\tau_{all}> T \text{ or } \taumaxminus(3/4)\leq T} \text{ and } \min\{\taumaxminus(3/4),\taubetaminus,\taupsiplus\}>T]

\displaystyle+\Pr\quantity[\min\{\taumaxminus(3/4),\taubetaminus,\taupsiplus\}\leq T]

\displaystyle\leq\Pr\quantity[\tau>T]+\Pr\quantity[\taumaxminus(3/4)\leq T \text{ and } \min\{\taubetaminus,\taupsiplus\}>T]+\Pr\quantity[\min\{\taubetaminus,\taupsiplus\}\leq T]

\displaystyle\leq 1/n.

∎

Proof of
Lemma

5.21
(
Item

4
).

Let
\tau=\min\{\tau_{\mathrm{cons}},\tau_{\beta}^{-}\}
.
Let
u_{t}=1-\alpha_{t}(1)
. Then,

\displaystyle\E_{t-1}[u_{t}]=1-\alpha_{t-1}(1)\quantity(1+1+\alpha_{t-1}(1)-2\beta_{t-1})=u_{t-1}\quantity(1-\alpha_{t-1}(1))\leq u_{t-1}\quantity(1-\frac{1}{3}).

Let
r=1-\frac{1}{3}=\frac{2}{3}
,
X_{t}=r^{-t}u_{t}
, and
Y_{t}=X_{t\wedge\tau}
.
Then,

\displaystyle\E_{t-1}[Y_{t}]-Y_{t-1}=\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}\quantity(\E_{t-1}[X_t]-X_{t-1})_{t-1}[X_{t}]-X_{t-1}\leq\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}r^{-t}\quantity(r^{-t}\E_{t-1}[u_t]-r^{-(t-1)}u_{t-1})_{t-1}[u_{t}]-r^{-(t-1)}u_{t-1}\leq 0,

i.e.,
Y_{t}
is a submartingale.
Hence, we have
\E[Y_{T}]\leq\E[Y_{0}]=u_{0}\leq 1
and

\displaystyle\E[Y_{T}]\geq\E[X_{T}\mid\tau>T]\Pr[\tau>T]=r^{-T}\E[u_{T}\mid\tau>T]\Pr[\tau>T]\geq r^{-T}n^{-1}\Pr[\tau>T].

Consequently, we have

\displaystyle\Pr[\tau>T]\leq nr^{T}\leq n\exp\quantity(-\frac{T}{3})\leq 1/n^{2}.

Thus, from
Lemmas

5.2
and
5.3
, we have

\displaystyle\Pr[\tau_{\mathrm{cons}}>T]

\displaystyle\leq\Pr[\tau>T]+\Pr[\tau_{\beta}^{-}\leq T]

\displaystyle\leq\Pr[\tau>T]+\Pr[\tau_{\beta}^{-}\leq T\text{ and }\tau_{\psi}^{+}>T]+\Pr[\tau_{\psi}^{+}\leq T]

\displaystyle\leq 1/n.

∎

Proof of
Lemma

5.20
.

We have the following:

•

From
Lemma

5.21
(
Item

1
), for some
T_{1}=O\quantity(\log n/\npm_0)
, we have
\alpha_{T_{1}}(1)\geq(1-c^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}})\beta_{T_{1}}
,
\beta_{T_{1}}\geq 1/2-x_{\beta}
, and
\psi_{T_{1}}\leq x_{\psi}
, with probability at least
1-n^{-10}
.

•

From
Lemma

5.21
(
Item

2
), for some
T_{2}=O(\log n)
, we have
\alpha_{T_{1}+T_{2}}(1)\geq 1-4c^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}}
,
\beta_{T_{1}+T_{2}}\geq 1/2-x_{\beta}
, and
\psi_{T_{1}+T_{2}}\leq x_{\psi}
, with probability at least
1-n^{-10}
.

•

Assume that
c^{\downarrow}_{\widetilde{\max}}=1/32
. From
Lemma

5.21
(
Item

3
), for some
T_{3}=O(\log n)
, we have
\alpha_{T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}}(1)=\beta_{T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}}
and
\alpha_{T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}}(1)\geq 3/4
with probability at least
1-1/n^{2}
.

•

From
Lemma

5.21
(
Item

4
), for some
T_{4}=O(\log n)
, we have
\alpha_{T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}+T_{4}}(1)=1
with probability at least
1-1/n^{2}
.

Thus, we obtain the claim.
∎

5.8
Putting All Together

Lemma 5.23
.

Let
C>0
be any constant.
For some

\displaystyle T=\begin{cases}O\quantity(\log n)&\quantity(\text{if } k\le\frac{C\sqrt{n}}{(\log n)^2}),\\
O\quantity(\sqrt{n}(\log n)^3)&(\text{otherwise}),\end{cases}

(25)

we have

\displaystyle\Pr\quantity[\psi_T\leq\xpsi\text{ and } \beta_T\geq\frac{1}{2}-\xbeta\text{ and } \npm_T\geq\frac{(\log n)^{1.5}}{\sqrt{n}}]\geq 1-p_{\bot}-O\quantity(\frac{\log n}{n}).

Proof.

Combining
Lemmas

5.2
,
5.3
and
5.4
, we have the following:

1.

From
Lemma

5.3
(
Item

1
) and the definition of
p_{\bot}
,
\psi_{1}\leq x_{\psi}
and
\beta_{1}\geq 1/n
hold with probability at least
1-p_{\bot}-n^{-\Omega(1)}
.

2.

From
Lemma

5.2
(
Items

1
and
2
),
for some
T=O(\log n)
,
\beta_{1+T}\geq 1/2-x_{\beta}
holds with probability at least
1-O(\log n/n)
.

3.

From
Lemma

5.3
(
Item

2
),
\max_{t\in[n^{2}]}\psi_{t}\leq x_{\psi}
holds with probability at least
1-n^{-10}
.

4.

From
Lemma

5.4
, for some
T^{\prime}=O\quantity(\sqrt{n}(\log n)^3)
,
\gamma_{1+T+T^{\prime}}\geq(\log n)^{2}/\sqrt{n}
,
\psi_{1+T+T^{\prime}}\leq x_{\psi}
, and
\beta_{1+T+T^{\prime}}\geq 1/2-x_{\beta}
with probability at least
1-n^{-\Omega(1)}
.

Since
\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t}\geq\gamma_{t}/\beta_{t}\geq\gamma_{t}
holds for any
t
, combining
Items

1
,
2
,
3
and
4
, we obtain the claim for general
k
.

For the case where
k=O\quantity(\sqrt{n}/(\log n)^2)
,

\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t}\geq\beta_{t}/k=\Omega(\beta_{t}(\log n)^{2}/\sqrt{n})
holds for any
t
.
Hence, from
Items

1
,
2
and
3
, we obtain the claim.

∎

Lemma 5.24
.

Suppose that
\psi_{0}\leq x_{\psi}
,
\beta_{0}\geq 1/2-x_{\beta}
, and
\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}=\omega(\log n/\sqrt{n})
.
Then,
\tau_{\mathrm{cons}}=O\quantity(\log n/\npm_0)
with high probability.

Proof.

We have the following:

•

From
Lemma

5.16
(
Item

1
), for some
T_{1}=O\quantity(\log n/\npm_0)
, we have
\min_{j\neq I_{T_{1}}}\eta_{T_{1}}(j)\geq x_{\eta}
,
\beta_{T_{1}}\geq 1/2-x_{\beta}
,
\psi_{T_{1}}\leq x_{\psi}
, and
\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{T_{1}}=\Omega(\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0})=\omega(\log n/\sqrt{n})
with probability at least
1-n^{-10}
.

•

From
Lemma

5.20
, for some
T_{2}=O\quantity(\log n/\npm_0)
, we have
\tau_{\mathrm{cons}}\leq T_{1}+T_{2}
with probability at least
1-1/n
.

Thus, we obtain the claim.
∎

Proof of
Theorem

1.1
.

From
Lemma

5.23
, we have that
\psi_{T}\leq x_{\psi}
and
\beta_{T}\geq 1/2-x_{\beta}
and
\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{T}\geq(\log n)^{1.5}/\sqrt{n}
hold with high probability for some
T
as defined in
Eq.

25
(
Lemma

5.23
).
Then, from
Lemma

5.24
, we reach a consensus within additional
O\quantity(\frac{\log n}{\npm_T})=O\quantity( \min\qty{ k\log n, \sqrt{n/\log n} } )
rounds with high probability.
Here, we use
\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{T}\geq\frac{\beta_{T}}{k}=\Omega(k)
if
k
is small and
\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{T}\geq(\log n)^{1.5}/\sqrt{n}
if
k
is large.
Therefore, the consensus time is bounded by

\displaystyle\tau_{\mathrm{cons}}\leq T+O\quantity( \min\qty{ k\log n, \sqrt{n/\log n} } )=\widetilde{O}(\min\quantity{k, \sqrt{n}})

and obtain the claim.
∎

5.9
Lower Bound

In this subsection, we prove
Theorem

1.3
.

Lemma 5.25
.

If
\beta_{0}\geq 1/2-x_{\beta}
,
\psi_{0}\leq x_{\psi}
, and
\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}\geq C\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}
for a sufficiently large constant
C>0
, then
\tau_{\mathrm{cons}}=\Omega(1/\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0})
with high probability.

Proof.

Let
T=\frac{C_{\mbox{\tiny\ref{lem:hitting time for tnpm and npm}(\ref{item:taualphamaxup})}}}{\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}}
.
From
Lemma

5.6
(
Item

2
) and
Lemma

5.2
(
Item

3
), we have

\displaystyle\Pr\quantity[\taucons\leq T]

\displaystyle\leq\Pr\quantity[\taumaxup\leq T]

\displaystyle\leq\Pr\quantity[\taumaxup\leq T \text{ and } \taubetaminus> T]+\Pr\quantity[\taubetaminus\leq T]

\displaystyle\leq\Pr\quantity[\taumaxup\leq\min\{T,\taubetaminus\}]+\Pr\quantity[\taubetaminus\leq T]

\displaystyle\leq k\underbrace{\exp\quantity(-\Omega\qty(\frac{\npm_0n}{T}))}_{=\exp(-\Omega((\npm_0)^2 n))}+T\exp\quantity(-\Omega\qty(n\xbeta^2))

\displaystyle\leq 1/n.

∎

Proof of
Theorem

1.3
.

For a constant
c>0
such that
k\leq(1/2-c)\cdot n
, define

k^{*}=\frac{2\sqrt{n}}{c\log n}.

Suppose that
k\leq k^{*}
.
Consider the initial configuration defined by

\alpha_{0}(i)=\frac{1}{2k}\text{ for all }i\in[k].

Then,
\beta_{0}=1/2
,
\psi_{0}\leq 0
, and
\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}=\frac{1}{2k}\geq\Omega\quantity(\frac{\log n}{\sqrt{n}})
.
Therefore, from
Lemma

5.25
, with high probability,
\tau_{\mathrm{cons}}=\Omega(1/\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0})=\Omega(k)
.

Suppose that
k>k^{*}
.
Consider the initial configuration defined by

\displaystyle\alpha_{0}(i)=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{2k^{*}}-\frac{k-k^{*}}{nk^{*}}&\text{if }i\in[k^{*}],\\
\frac{1}{n}&\text{if }k^{*}<i\leq k.\end{cases}

Then,
\beta_{0}=1/2
,
\psi_{0}\leq 0
, and

\displaystyle\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2k^{*}}\quantity(1 - \frac{2k-2k^*}{n})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2k^{*}}\quantity(1 - \frac{2k}{n})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{c}{2k^{*}}

\displaystyle\because k\leq(1/2-c)\cdot n

\displaystyle=\Omega\quantity(\frac{\log n}{\sqrt{n}}).

Therefore, from
Lemma

5.25
, with high probability,
\tau_{\mathrm{cons}}=\Omega(1/\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{0})=\Omega(k^{*})=\widetilde{\Omega}(\sqrt{n})
.
∎

6
Analysis for the Population Protocol Model

Throughout this section, we recall quantities and hitting times defined in
Definitions

4.1
and
4.16
.
We frequently use results from
Section

4.2
.

The key distinction from the gossip model is that, in the population protocol model, we introduce a
margin
to the parameters in
\tau_{\beta}^{-}
and
\tau_{\psi}^{+}
.
In the gossip model, we can show that once
\beta_{t}
exceeds the threshold
1/2-x_{\beta}
, it never drops below this threshold due to concentration in every round.
In contrast, in the population protocol model, even after
\beta_{t}
exceeds
1/2-x_{\beta}
, it is still possible for it to fall below the threshold.
To address this, we instead prove that
\beta_{t}
does not fall below a lower threshold,
1/2-2x_{\beta}
, for a sufficiently long period of time (
Item

3
of
Lemma

6.1
).
We also introduce a similar margin for the parameter
x_{\psi}
in the definition of
\tau_{\psi}^{+}(x)
(
Item

2
of
Lemma

6.2
).

For clarity and readability, we present complete proofs for the population protocol model separately from those for the gossip model, including all necessary lemmas and arguments. While the two models share a common high-level structure, interleaving their proofs would obscure the exposition and hinder readability.

6.1
Behavior of the Fraction of Decided Vertices

Consider the stopping times defined in
Definition

4.16
.
In this section, we present the following two lemmas:
(i)
\beta_{t}
reaches at least
1/2-x
within
O(n\log n)
steps and then stays at least
1/2-O(x)
for a sufficiently long period (
Lemma

6.1
); and
(ii)
\psi_{t}
drops to at most
x
within
O(n\log n)
steps and then remains at most
O(x)
for a sufficiently long period (
Lemma

6.2
).
Specifically, we intend to apply the following results for
x
such that
x=\omega(\sqrt{\log n/n})
and
x=o(\log n/\sqrt{n})
.

Lemma 6.1

(Growth of
\beta_{t}
)
.

Let
x=x(n)
be an arbitrary positive function such that
x=\omega(\sqrt{\log n/n})
and
x=o(\log n/\sqrt{n})
.
We have the following:

1.

Let
C>0
be an arbitrary constant.
Suppose that
\beta_{0}>0
.
Then, for some
\ell=O(\log n)
,

\beta_{\ell n}\geq\frac{C\log n}{n}
with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
.

2.

For some
\ell=O(\log n)
,

\Pr[\tau_{\beta}^{+}(x)>\ell n]\leq\ell\quantity(\exp\qty(-\Omega\qty(n\beta_0))+\exp\qty(-\Omega\qty(nx^2))).

3.

Suppose
\beta_{0}\geq 1/2-x
.
Then, for any
T>0
,
\Pr\quantity[\tau_\beta^-(2x)\leq T]\leq T\exp\quantity(-\Omega\qty(nx^2))
.

Lemma 6.2

(Decay of
\psi_{t}
)
.

Let
x=x(n)
be an arbitrary positive function such that
x=\omega(\sqrt{\log n/n})
and
x=o(\log n/\sqrt{n})
.
We have the following:

1.

Suppose
\psi_{0}\geq x
.
Then, for some
\ell=O(\log n)
,

\Pr[\tau_{\psi}^{-}(x)>\ell n]\leq\ell\exp\quantity(-\Omega\qty(nx^2))
.

2.

Suppose
\psi_{0}\leq x
.
Then, for any
T\geq 1
,
\Pr[\tau_{\psi}^{+}(2x)\leq T]\leq T\exp\quantity(-\Omega\qty(nx^2))
.

Proof of
Item

1
of
Lemma

6.1
.

For positive constants
c_{\beta}^{\uparrow},c_{\beta}^{\downarrow}\in(0,1)
, let
\tau_{\beta}^{\uparrow}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\inf\{t\geq 0:\beta_{t}\geq(1+c_{\beta}^{\uparrow})\beta_{0}\}
and
\tau_{\beta}^{\downarrow}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\inf\{t\geq 0:\beta_{t}\leq(1-c_{\beta}^{\downarrow})\beta_{0}\}
.
For
y\leq 1/2-c
, let
\tau=\min\{\tau_{\beta}^{\uparrow},\tau_{\beta}^{\downarrow},\tau_{\beta}^{+}(y)\}
.
Then, for
\tau>t-1
, we have

\displaystyle\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]=\beta_{t-1}+\frac{\beta_{t-1}\quantity(1-2\beta_{t-1})+\gamma_{t-1}}{n}\geq\beta_{t-1}+\frac{2c(1-c_{\beta}^{\downarrow})\beta_{0}}{n}.

(26)

Note that we use
Lemma

4.10
(
Item

1
) in the first equality.

Hence, by setting
X_{t}=\beta_{t}
and
R=\frac{2c(1-c_{\beta}^{\downarrow})\beta_{0}}{n}>0
, we obtain

\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}X_{t-1}+R-\quantity(X_{t-1}+\drift-\E_{t-1}[X_t])_{t-1}[X_{t}]=\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}\beta_{t-1}+R-\quantity(\beta_{t-1}+\drift-\E_{t-1}[\beta_t])_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]\leq 0.

Moreover,
\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}\quantity(\E_{t-1}[X_t]-X_t)_{t-1}[X_{t}]-X_{t}=\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}\quantity(\E_{t-1}[\beta_t]-\beta_t)_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]-\beta_{t}
satisfies
\quantity(\frac{1}{n},\frac{(1+c_\beta^\uparrow)\beta_0}{n^2})
-Bernstein condition.
Note that we use
Lemma

4.10
(
Item

3
),
\mathbf{1}_{\tau>t-1}\beta_{t-1}\leq(1+c_{\beta}^{\downarrow})\beta_{0}
, and
Lemma

3.7
(
Items

2
and
3
).

Applying
Lemma

3.11
(
Item

2
) with
I^{+}=(1+c_{\beta}^{\uparrow})\beta_{0}
,
I^{-}=(1-c_{\beta}^{\downarrow})\beta_{0}
, and

T^{\prime}=\frac{(1+\varepsilon)(I^{+}-X_{0})}{R}=\frac{(1+\varepsilon)c_{\beta}^{\uparrow}}{2c(1-c_{\beta}^{\downarrow})}n=\Theta(n)
for an arbitrary constant
\varepsilon>0
, we have

\displaystyle\Pr\quantity[\min\{\tau_\beta^\uparrow,\taubetaplus(y)\} >T']\leq\exp\quantity(-\Omega\qty(\frac{\beta_0^2}{\frac{\beta_0}{n^2}\cdot n+\frac{\beta_0}{n}}))\leq\exp\quantity(-\Omega\qty(n\beta_0)).

(27)

We apply
Lemma

A.6
for
Z_{t}=\mathrm{opn}_{t}
,
T=T^{\prime}
,
\varphi(Z_{t})=\sqrt{n\beta_{t}}
,
\tau=\tau_{\beta}^{+}(y)
,
c_{\varphi}^{\uparrow}=c_{\beta}^{\uparrow}
,
x_{0}=1
, and
x^{*}=\sqrt{C\log n}
. From these settings, we have

\tau^{+}_{\varphi}(x_{0})=\inf\{t\geq 0:\sqrt{n\beta_{t}}\geq 1\}=0
,

\tau^{+}_{\varphi}(x_{*})=\inf\{t\geq 0:\beta_{t}\geq C\log n/n\}
, and

\tau^{\uparrow}_{\varphi}=\inf\{t\geq 0:\sqrt{n\beta_{t}}\geq(1+c_{\varphi}^{\uparrow})\sqrt{n\beta_{0}}\}=\tau_{\beta}^{\uparrow}
.
Hence,

\displaystyle\Pr\quantity[\min\qty{\tauphiplus(x_0), \tau}\leq T']=1

holds, i.e., the first condition of
Lemma

A.6
is satisfied for
C_{1}=1
.
Next, for any configuration of
\beta_{0}\leq C\log n/n
, we have

\displaystyle\Pr\quantity[\min\qty{\tauphiup, \tau}>T']\geq 1-\exp\quantity(-\Omega(n\beta_0))=1-\exp\quantity(-\Omega(\varphi(Z_0)^2)).

i.e., the second condition of
Lemma

A.6
is satisfied for some positive constant
C_{2}>0
.
Note that we use
Eq.

27
in the second inequality.

Thus, from
Lemma

A.6
with
\varepsilon=n^{-10}
,
for some
\ell=O(\log n)
,

\Pr\quantity[\tauphiplus(x_*)>\ell T']\leq n^{-10}.

∎

Proof of
Item

2
of
Lemma

6.1
.

Let
c
be an arbitrary constant such that
0<c<1/2
.
