\mathbf{Cov}_{t-1}\quantity[\alpha_t(i),\beta_t]\leq\frac{\alpha_{t-1}(i)}{n}
.

Proof.

From
B.2
and
Lemma

4.2
(
Item

2
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{Cov}_{t-1}\quantity[\alpha_t(i),\beta_t]

\displaystyle=\Var_{t-1}\quantity[\alpha_t(i)]+\sum_{j\in[k]\setminus\{i\}}\mathbf{Cov}_{t-1}\quantity[\alpha_t(i),\alpha_t(j)]

\displaystyle=\Var_{t-1}\quantity[\alpha_t(i)]-\frac{(1-\beta_{t-1})\alpha_{t-1}(i)(\beta_{t-1}-\alpha_{t-1}(i))}{n}

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha_{t-1}(i)}{n}\quantity[(1-\beta_{t-1})(1-\alpha_{t-1}(i))+\alpha_{t-1}(i)(\beta_{t-1}-\alpha_{t-1}(i))]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\alpha_{t-1}(i)}{n}.

∎

Since
\Var_{t-1}\quantity[\beta_{t-1}]=\sum_{i\in[k]}\sum_{j\in[k]}\Cov_{t-1}\quantity[\alpha_t(i), \alpha_t(j)]=\sum_{i\in[k]}\Cov_{t-1}\quantity[\alpha_t(i), \beta_t]
, we obtain

\displaystyle\Var_{t-1}\quantity[\beta_{t-1}]

\displaystyle=\frac{(1-\beta_{t-1})(\beta_{t-1}-\gamma_{t-1})+\beta_{t-1}\gamma_{t-1}-\norm{\alpha_{t-1}}_{3}^{3}}{n}

\displaystyle=\frac{(\beta_{t-1}-\gamma_{t-1})(1-\beta_{t-1}+\gamma_{t-1})+\gamma_{t-1}^{2}-\norm{\alpha_{t-1}}_{3}^{3}}{n}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\beta_{t-1}}{n}.

Note that
\gamma_{t-1}^{2}\leq\beta_{t-1}\norm{\alpha_{t-1}}_{3}^{3}
holds from the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality.
Hence, the result follows.

(iii) Bernstein condition.

Write
Y_{t}(v)=\frac{1}{n}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathrm{opn}_{t}(v)\neq\bot}-\E_{t-1}[\mathbf{1}_{\mathrm{opn}_{t}(v)\neq\bot}])
for convenience.
From
Item

1
of
Lemma

3.7
,
Y_{t}(v)
satisfies
\frac{1}{n},\quantity(\frac{1}{n}, \Var_{t-1}[Y_t(v)])_{t-1}[Y_{t}(v)]
-Bernstein condition.
From definition, we have

\beta_{t}-\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]=\sum_{v\in V}Y_{t}(v)
, i.e.,
\beta_{t}-\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]
conditioned on
t-1
round is the sum of
n
independent random variables
(Y_{t}(v))_{v\in V}
.
Hence, by
Item

5
of
Lemma

3.7
,
\beta_{t}-\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]
satisfies
\frac{1}{n},\sum_{v\in V}\quantity(\frac{1}{n}, \sum_{v\in V}\Var_{t-1}[Y_t(v)])_{t-1}[Y_{t}(v)]
-Bernstein condition.
Since

\displaystyle\sum_{v\in V}\Var_{t-1}[Y_{t}(v)]

\displaystyle=\Var[\beta_{t}]\leq\frac{\beta_{t-1}(i)}{n}

holds from
Lemma

4.3
(
Item

2
), we obtain the claim.
∎

Proof of
Lemma

4.4
.

(i) Expectation.

From
Item

1
of
Lemma

4.2
, we have

\displaystyle\E_{t-1}\quantity[\delta_t^{(\epsilon)}]

\displaystyle=\alpha_{t-1}(i)\quantity(\alpha_{t-1}(i)+2(1-\beta_{t-1}))-(1+\varepsilon)\alpha_{t-1}(j)\quantity(\alpha_{t-1}(j)+2(1-\beta_{t-1}))

\displaystyle=\quantity(\alpha_{t-1}(i)-(1+\varepsilon)\alpha_{t-1}(j))\quantity(\alpha_{t-1}(i)+\alpha_{t-1}(j)+2(1-\beta_{t-1}))+\varepsilon\alpha_{t-1}(i)\alpha_{t-1}(j).

(ii) Variance.

From
Items

2
and
B.2
of
Lemma

4.2
, we have

\displaystyle\Var_{t-1}\quantity[\delta_t]=\Var_{t-1}\quantity[\alpha_t(i)]+\Var_{t-1}\quantity[\alpha_t(j)]-2\Cov_{t-1}\quantity[\alpha_t(i),\alpha_t(j)]\geq\frac{(1-\beta_{t-1})^{2}}{n}\quantity(\alpha_{t-1}(i)+\alpha_{t-1}(j)).

(iii) Bernstein condition.

From
Lemma

4.2
(
Item

3
) and
Lemma

3.7
(
Item

3
), for any
l\in[k]
and
x\geq 0
,

x\cdot\alpha_{t}(l)-\quantity(\alpha_t(l)-\E_{t-1}[\alpha_t(l)])_{t-1}[\alpha_{t}(l)]
satisfies
\quantity(\frac{x}{n},\frac{x^2\alpha_{t-1}(i)}{n})
-Bernstein condition.
Since

\delta_{t}^{(\varepsilon)}-\E_{t-1}\quantity[\delta_t^{(\epsilon)}]=\alpha_{t}(i)-\quantity(\alpha_t(i)-\E_{t-1}[\alpha_t(i)])_{t-1}[\alpha_{t}(i)]-(1+\varepsilon)\alpha_{t}(j)-\quantity(\alpha_t(j)-\E_{t-1}[\alpha_t(j)])_{t-1}[\alpha_{t}(j)],

We obtain the claim by
Lemma

3.7
(
Item

2
) and
Lemma

3.8
(
Item

1
).
∎

Proof of
Lemma

4.5
.

(i) Expectation Upper Bound.

First, we observe that

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in[k]}\E_{t-1}[\alpha_{t}(i)]^{2}=4(1-\beta_{t-1})^{2}\gamma_{t-1}+4(1-\beta_{t-1})\norm{\alpha_t}_{3}^{3}+\norm{\alpha_t}_{4}^{4}

(39)

holds from
Item

1
of
Lemma

4.2
.
Hence,

\displaystyle\E_{t-1}[\gamma_{t}]

\displaystyle=4(1-\beta_{t-1})^{2}\gamma_{t-1}+4(1-\beta_{t-1})\norm{\alpha_t}_{3}^{3}+\norm{\alpha_t}_{4}^{4}+\sum_{i\in[k]}\Var_{t-1}[\alpha_{t}(i)]\leq 10\gamma_{t-1}.

holds. Note that
\sum_{i\in[k]}\Var_{t-1}[\alpha_{t}(i)]\leq\frac{\beta_{t-1}}{n}
from
Item

2
of
Lemma

4.2
and
\beta_{t-1}\leq n\gamma_{t-1}
holds.

(ii) Expectation Lower Bound.

From the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have

\displaystyle\gamma_{t}^{2}=\quantity(\sum_{i\in[k]}\alpha_t(i)^{0.5}\alpha_t(i)^{1.5})^{2}\leq\beta_{t}\norm{\alpha_t}_{3}^{3}\quad\text{and}\quad\norm{\alpha_t}_{3}^{6}=\quantity(\sum_{i\in[k]}\alpha_t(i)\alpha_t(i)^{2})^{2}\leq\gamma_{t}\norm{\alpha_t}_{4}^{4}.

Hence, from
Eq.

39
and
Lemma

4.3
(
Item

1
), we have

\displaystyle\beta_{t-1}^{2}\gamma_{t-1}\sum_{i\in[k]}\E_{t-1}[\alpha_{t}(i)]^{2}

\displaystyle\geq 4(1-\beta_{t-1})^{2}\beta_{t-1}^{2}\gamma_{t-1}^{2}+4(1-\beta_{t-1})\beta_{t-1}^{2}\gamma_{t-1}\norm{\np_t}_{3}^{3}+\beta_{t-1}^{2}\gamma_{t-1}\norm{\np_{t-1}}_{4}^{4}

\displaystyle\geq 4(1-\beta_{t-1})^{2}\beta_{t-1}^{2}\gamma_{t-1}^{2}+4(1-\beta_{t-1})\beta_{t-1}\gamma_{t-1}^{3}+\gamma_{t-1}^{4}

\displaystyle=\gamma_{t-1}^{2}\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]^{2}.

Furthermore,
\Var_{t-1}[\alpha_{t}(i)]\geq\frac{\quantity(1-\beta_{t-1})^{2}\alpha_{t-1}(i)}{n}
from
Lemma

4.2
(
Item

2
), we obtain

\displaystyle\beta_{t-1}^{2}\E_{t-1}[\gamma_{t}]=\beta_{t-1}^{2}\sum_{i\in[k]}\quantity(\sum_{i\in[k]} \E_{t-1}[\alpha_t(i)]^2+\sum_{i\in[k]} \Var_{t-1}[\alpha_t(i)])_{t-1}[\alpha_{t}(i)]^{2}+\sum_{i\in[k]}\Var_{t-1}[\alpha_{t}(i)]\geq\gamma_{t-1}\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]^{2}+\frac{\beta_{t-1}^{3}(1-\beta_{t-1})^{2}}{n}.

(iii) Bernstein condition.

Write

Z_{t}(v,u)=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathrm{opn}_{t}(v)=\mathrm{opn}_{t}(u)}\mathbf{1}_{\mathrm{opn}_{t}(v)\neq\bot}-\quantity(\indicator_{\opn_t(v)=\opn_t(u)}\indicator_{\opn_t(v)\neq\bot}-\E_{t-1}[\indicator_{\opn_t(v)=\opn_t(u)}\indicator_{\opn_t(v)\neq\bot}])_{t-1}[\mathbf{1}_{\mathrm{opn}_{t}(v)=\mathrm{opn}_{t}(u)}\mathbf{1}_{\mathrm{opn}_{t}(v)\neq\bot}]

for convenience.
From
Item

1
of
Lemma

3.7
,
Z_{t}(v,u)
satisfies
\frac{1}{n^{2}},\quantity(\frac{1}{n^2}, \Var_{t-1}[Z_t(v,u)])_{t-1}[Z_{t}(v,u)]
-Bernstein condition.
Observe that
\gamma_{t}-\E_{t-1}[\gamma_{t}]=\sum_{v\in V}\sum_{u\in V}Z_{t}(v,u)
.
In other words,
\gamma_{t}-\E_{t-1}[\gamma_{t}]
conditioned on
t-1
round is the sum of
n^{2}
random variables
(Z_{t}(v,u))_{v,u\in V}
.
Furthermore,
(Z_{t}(v,u))_{v,u\in V}
is a read-
2n-1
family of independent random variables
(\mathrm{opn}_{t}(v))_{v\in V}
.
Thus, from
Item

2
of
Lemma

3.7
,
\gamma_{t}-\E_{t-1}[\gamma_{t}]
satisfies
\frac{2n-1}{n^{2}},(2n-1)\sum_{v,u\in V}\quantity(\frac{2n-1}{n^2}, (2n-1)\sum_{v,u\in V}\Var_{t-1}[Z_t(v,u)])_{t-1}[Z_{t}(v,u)]
-Bernstein condition.
Now, we bound
\sum_{v,u\in V}\Var_{t-1}[Z_{t}(v,u)]
.
From
Lemma

4.5
(
Item

1
), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{v,u\in V}\Var_{t-1}[Z_{t}(v,u)]\leq\frac{1}{n^{4}}\sum_{v,u\in V}\E_{t-1}[\mathbf{1}_{\mathrm{opn}_{t}(v)=\mathrm{opn}_{t}(u)}\mathbf{1}_{\mathrm{opn}_{t}(v)\neq\bot}]=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\E_{t-1}[\gamma_{t}]\leq\frac{10\gamma_{t-1}}{n^{2}},

and we obtain the result.
∎

Proof of
Lemma

4.6
.

(i) Expectation.

From definition of
\psi_{t}
and
Lemma

4.3
(
Item

1
), we have

\displaystyle\E_{t-1}[\psi_{t}]=2\E_{t-1}\quantity[\beta_t]^{2}-\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]-\E_{t-1}[\gamma_{t}]+\Var_{t-1}[\beta_{t}],

\displaystyle\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]=\beta_{t-1}-\psi_{t-1},

\displaystyle 2\beta_{t-1}^{2}-\gamma_{t-1}=\beta_{t-1}+\psi_{t-1}.

Applying
Lemma

4.8
(
Item

2
), we have

\displaystyle\beta_{t-1}^{2}2\quantity(2\E_{t-1}\qty[\beta_t]^2-\E_{t-1}[\beta_t]-\E_{t-1}[\gamma_t])_{t-1}\quantity[\beta_t]^{2}-\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]-\E_{t-1}[\gamma_{t}]

\displaystyle\leq\beta_{t-1}^{2}2\quantity(2\E_{t-1}\qty[\beta_t]^2-\E_{t-1}[\beta_t])_{t-1}\quantity[\beta_t]^{2}-\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]-\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]^{2}\gamma_{t-1}

\displaystyle=-\psi_{t-1}^{2}\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]

\displaystyle\leq 0.

Thus, from
Lemma

4.3
(
Item

2
), we obtain

\displaystyle\E_{t-1}[\psi_{t}]\leq\Var_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]\leq\frac{\beta_{t-1}}{n}.

(ii) Bernstein condition.

First, we show the following claim:

Claim B.4
.

\beta_{t}^{2}-\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}^{2}]
satisfies
\quantity(\frac{2}{n},\frac{20\beta_{t-1}^2}{n})
-Bernstein condition.

Proof.

Write

Y_{t}(v,u)=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathrm{opn}_{t}(v)\neq\bot}\mathbf{1}_{\mathrm{opn}_{t}(u)\neq\bot}-\quantity(\indicator_{\opn_t(v)\neq\bot}\indicator_{\opn_t(u)\neq\bot}-\E_{t-1}[\indicator_{\opn_t(v)\neq\bot}\indicator_{\opn_t(u)\neq\bot}])_{t-1}[\mathbf{1}_{\mathrm{opn}_{t}(v)\neq\bot}\mathbf{1}_{\mathrm{opn}_{t}(u)\neq\bot}]

for convenience.
From
Item

1
of
Lemma

3.7
,
Y_{t}(v,u)
satisfies
\frac{1}{n^{2}},\quantity(\frac{1}{n^2}, \Var_{t-1}[Y_t(v,u)])_{t-1}[Y_{t}(v,u)]
-Bernstein condition.
Observe that
\beta_{t}^{2}-\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}^{2}]=\sum_{v\in V}\sum_{u\in V}Y_{t}(v,u)
.
In other words,
\beta_{t}^{2}-\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}^{2}]
conditioned on
t-1
round is the sum of
n^{2}
random variables
(Y_{t}(v,u))_{v,u\in V}
.
Furthermore,
(Y_{t}(v,u))_{v,u\in V}
is a read-
2n-1
family of independent random variables
(\mathrm{opn}_{t}(v))_{v\in V}
.
Thus, from
Item

2
of
Lemma

3.7
,
\beta_{t}^{2}-\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}^{2}]
satisfies
\frac{2n-1}{n^{2}},(2n-1)\sum_{v,u\in V}\quantity(\frac{2n-1}{n^2}, (2n-1)\sum_{v,u\in V}\Var_{t-1}[Y_t(v,u)])_{t-1}[Y_{t}(v,u)]
-Bernstein condition.
Now, we bound
\sum_{v,u\in V}\Var_{t-1}[Y_{t}(v,u)]
.
From
Lemma

4.3
(
Items

1
and
2
), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{v,u\in V}\Var_{t-1}[Y_{t}(v,u)]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n^{4}}\sum_{v,u\in V}\E_{t-1}[\mathbf{1}_{\mathrm{opn}_{t}(v)\neq\bot}\mathbf{1}_{\mathrm{opn}_{t}(u)\neq\bot}]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}^{2}]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\quantity(\E_{t-1}[\npo_t]^2+\Var_{t-1}[\npo_t])_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]^{2}+\Var_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n^{2}}\quantity(9\npo_{t-1}^2+\frac{\npo_{t-1}}{n})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{10\beta_{t-1}^{2}}{n^{2}}.

holds, and we obtain the claim.
∎

From definition,
\psi_{t}-\E_{t-1}[\psi_{t}]=2\beta_{t}^{2}-\quantity(\npo_t^2-\E_{t-1}[\npo_t^2])_{t-1}[\beta_{t}^{2}]+\quantity(\E_{t-1}[\npo_t]-\npo_t)_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]-\beta_{t}+\quantity(\E_{t-1}[\npt_t]-\npt_t)_{t-1}[\gamma_{t}]-\gamma_{t}
.
Combining
Items

1
and
3
of
Lemma

3.7
,

2\beta_{t}^{2}-\quantity(\npo_t^2-\E_{t-1}[\npo_t^2])_{t-1}[\beta_{t}^{2}]+\quantity(\E_{t-1}[\npo_t]-\npo_t)_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]-\beta_{t}+\quantity(\E_{t-1}[\npt_t]-\npt_t)_{t-1}[\gamma_{t}]-\gamma_{t}

satisfies
\quantity(3\cdot\frac{8}{n}, 3\cdot\qty(\frac{80\npo_{t-1}}{n}+\frac{\npo_{t-1}}{n}+\frac{20\npo_{t-1}}{n}))
-Bernstein condition.
∎

Proof of
Lemma

4.7
.

(i) Expectation.

From
Lemmas

4.2
and
4.3
,

\displaystyle\frac{\E_{t-1}\quantity[\np_t(I_{t-1})]}{\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]}

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha_{t-1}(I_{t-1})}{\beta_{t-1}}1+\frac{\frac{\quantity(1+\frac{\frac{\E_{t-1}\qty[\np_t(I_{t-1})]}{\np_{t-1}(I_{t-1})}-\frac{\E_{t-1}[\npo_t]}{\npo_{t-1}}}{\frac{\E_{t-1}[\npo_t]}{\npo_{t-1}}})_{t-1}\quantity[\np_t(I_{t-1})]}{\alpha_{t-1}(I_{t-1})}-\frac{\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]}{\beta_{t-1}}}{\frac{\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]}{\beta_{t-1}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha_{t-1}(I_{t-1})}{\beta_{t-1}}\cdot\frac{\alpha_{t-1}(I_{t-1})+2(1-\beta_{t-1})}{2(1-\beta_{t-1})+\gamma_{t-1}/\beta_{t-1}}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}\quantity(1+\frac{\npm_{t-1}-\npt_{t-1}/\npo_{t-1}}{2(1-\npo_{t-1})+\npt_{t-1}/\npo_{t-1}})

(40)

holds.
Note that
I_{t-1}=\min\quantity{i\in[k]:\alpha_{t-1}(i)=\npm_{t-1}}=\min\quantity{i\in[k]:\tnp_{t-1}(i)=\tnpm_{t-1}}
.
Combining the above and
Lemma

B.1
(
Item

1
), we obtain

\displaystyle\E_{t-1}\quantity[\tnp_t(I_{t-1})]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\E_{t-1}\quantity[\np_t(I_{t-1})]}{\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]}-\frac{\mathbf{Cov}_{t-1}\quantity[\np_t(I_{t-1}),\npo_t]}{\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]^{2}}

\displaystyle\geq\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}\quantity(1+\frac{\npm_{t-1}-\npt_{t-1}/\npo_{t-1}}{2(1-\npo_{t-1})+\npt_{t-1}/\npo_{t-1}})-\frac{9\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}}{n}.

(41)

The last inequality holds from
B.3
(
\mathbf{Cov}_{t-1}\quantity[\np_t(i),\npo_t]\leq\alpha_{t-1}(i)/n
) and the assumptions of
\beta_{t-1}\geq 1/2-o(1)
,
\psi_{t-1}\leq o(1)
,
and
\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]=\beta_{t-1}-\psi_{t-1}\geq 1/2-o(1)\geq 1/3
.

(ii) Bernstein condition.

From
B.3
, we have

\Cov_{t-1}\quantity[\np_t(I_{t-1}),\npo_t]\leq\frac{\alpha_{t-1}(I_{t-1})}{n}=\frac{\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}}{n}
.
From
Eq.

40
and
Lemma

B.1
(
Item

2
), we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}\quantity(1+\frac{\npm_{t-1}-\npt_{t-1}/\npo_{t-1}}{2(1-\npo_{t-1})+\npt_{t-1}/\npo_{t-1}})-\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{t}-\frac{9\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}}{n}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\E_{t-1}\quantity[\np_t(I_{t-1})]}{\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]}-\widetilde{\alpha}_{t}(I_{t-1})-\frac{\Cov_{t-1}\quantity[\np_t(I_{t-1}),\npo_t]}{\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]^{2}}

\displaystyle(\text{from \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:expectation of tnpm lower bound key}})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\quantity(\E_{t-1}\qty[\np_t(I_{t-1})]-\np_t(I_{t-1}))_{t-1}\quantity[\np_t(I_{t-1})]-\alpha_{t}(I_{t-1})}{\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]}+\frac{\alpha_{t}(I_{t-1})\beta_{t}-\E_{t-1}\quantity[\np_t(I_{t-1})\npo_t]}{\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]^{2}}.

\displaystyle(\text{from \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:basic inequalities for ratio distribution} (\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{item:Bernstein condition of ratio distribution})})

Note that we use
\widetilde{\alpha}_{t}(I_{t-1})\leq\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{t}
in the second inequality.
From
Item

3
of
Lemma

4.2
and
Item

3
of
Lemma

3.7
, the random variable
\frac{2\quantity(\E_{t-1}\qty[\np_t(I_{t-1})]-\np_t(I_{t-1}))_{t-1}\quantity[\np_t(I_{t-1})]-\alpha_{t}(I_{t-1})}{\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]}
conditioned on round
t-1
satisfies
\frac{2}{n\quantity(\frac{2}{n\E_{t-1}[\npo_t]}, \frac{4\npm_{t-1}}{n\E_{t-1}[\npo_t]^2})_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]},\frac{4\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}}{n\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]^{2}}
-Bernstein condition.
We now check the Bernstein condition of
\alpha_{t}(i)\beta_{t}-\E_{t-1}[\alpha_{t}(i)\beta_{t}]
.

Claim B.5
.

For any
t\geq 1
and
i\in[k]
,

\alpha_{t}(i)\beta_{t}-\E_{t-1}[\alpha_{t}(i)\beta_{t}]
conditioned on round
t-1
satisfies
\quantity(\frac{2}{n}, \frac{14\np_{t-1}(i)\npo_{t-1}}{n})
-Bernstein condition.

Proof of
B.5
.

Write

X_{t}(v,u)=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathrm{opn}_{t}(v)=i}\mathbf{1}_{\mathrm{opn}_{t}(u)\neq\bot}-\quantity(\indicator_{\opn_t(v)=i}\indicator_{\opn_t(u)\neq\bot}-\E_{t-1}[\indicator_{\opn_t(v)=i}\indicator_{\opn_t(u)\neq\bot}])_{t-1}[\mathbf{1}_{\mathrm{opn}_{t}(v)=i}\mathbf{1}_{\mathrm{opn}_{t}(u)\neq\bot}]

for convenience.
From
Item

1
of
Lemma

3.7
, the random variable
X_{t}(v,u)
conditioned on round
t-1
satisfies
\frac{1}{n^{2}},\quantity(\frac{1}{n^2}, \Var_{t-1}[X_t(v,u)])_{t-1}[X_{t}(v,u)]
-Bernstein condition.
Observe that
\alpha_{t}(i)\beta_{t}-\E_{t-1}[\alpha_{t}(i)\beta_{t}]=\sum_{v\in V}\sum_{u\in V}X_{t}(v,u)
.
In other words,
\alpha_{t}(i)\beta_{t}-\E_{t-1}[\alpha_{t}(i)\beta_{t}]
conditioned on
t-1
round is the sum of
n^{2}
random variables
(X_{t}(v,u))_{v,u\in V}
.
Furthermore,
(X_{t}(v,u))_{v,u\in V}
is a read-
2n-1
family of independent random variables
(\mathrm{opn}_{t}(v))_{v\in V}
.
Thus, from
Item

2
of
Lemma

3.7
,
\alpha_{t}(i)\beta_{t}-\E_{t-1}[\alpha_{t}(i)\beta_{t}]
satisfies
\frac{2n-1}{n^{2}},(2n-1)\sum_{v,u\in V}\quantity(\frac{2n-1}{n^2}, (2n-1)\sum_{v,u\in V}\Var_{t-1}[X_t(v,u)])_{t-1}[X_{t}(v,u)]
-Bernstein condition.
Now, we bound the summation
\sum_{v,u\in V}\Var_{t-1}[X_{t}(v,u)]
as follows:
Since

\displaystyle\sum_{v,u\in V}\Var_{t-1}[X_{t}(v,u)]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n^{4}}\sum_{v,u\in V}\E_{t-1}[\mathbf{1}_{\mathrm{opn}_{t}(v)=i}\mathbf{1}_{\mathrm{opn}_{t}(u)\neq\bot}]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\E_{t-1}[\alpha_{t}(i)\beta_{t}]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\quantity(\E_{t-1}[\np_t(i)]\E_{t-1}[\npo_t]+\mathbf{Cov}_{t-1}\qty[\np_t(i),\npo_t])_{t-1}[\alpha_{t}(i)]\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]+\mathbf{Cov}_{t-1}\quantity[\np_t(i),\npo_t]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n^{2}}\quantity(6\np_{t-1}(i)\npo_{t-1}+\frac{\np_{t-1}(i)}{n})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{7\alpha_{t-1}(i)\beta_{t-1}}{n^{2}}

holds from
Lemmas

4.2
,
4.3
and
B.3
, we obtain the claim.
∎

From
B.5
and
Item

3
of
Lemma

3.7
, the random variable
\frac{\alpha_{t}(I_{t-1})\beta_{t}-\E_{t-1}\quantity[\np_t(I_{t-1})\npo_t]}{\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]^{2}}
conditioned on round
t-1
satisfies
\frac{2}{n\quantity(\frac{2}{n\E_{t-1}[\npo_t]^2}, \frac{14\npm_{t-1}\npo_{t-1}}{n\E_{t-1}[\npo_t]^4})_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]^{2}},\frac{14\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}\beta_{t-1}}{n\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]^{4}}
-Bernstein condition.
Therefore, from
Item

1
of
Lemma

3.7
and
Item

2
of
Lemma

3.7
, we obtain the claim.
Note that, from the assumptions of
\beta_{t-1}\geq 1/2-o(1)
and
\psi_{t-1}\leq o(1)
, we have
\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]=\beta_{t-1}-\psi_{t-1}\geq 1/2-o(1)\geq 1/3
.
∎

Proof of
Lemma

4.8
.

(i) Expectation.

From
Lemma

4.8
(
Item

2
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{\E_{t-1}\quantity[\npt_t]}{\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]^{2}}

\displaystyle\geq\widetilde{\gamma}_{t-1}+\frac{\beta_{t-1}(1-\beta_{t-1})^{2}}{n\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]^{2}}.

Hence, from
Lemma

B.1
(
Item

1
),
Lemma

4.3
(
Item

2
),
Lemma

4.5
(
Item

1
), and
B.6
(we will prove this later), we have

\displaystyle\E_{t-1}\quantity[\tnpt_t]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\E_{t-1}\quantity[\npt_t]}{\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}^{2}]}-\frac{\mathbf{Cov}_{t-1}\quantity[\npt_t,\npo_t^2]}{\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}^{2}]^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\E_{t-1}\quantity[\npt_t]}{\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]^{2}}-\frac{\E_{t-1}\quantity[\npt_t]\Var_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]}{\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}^{2}]\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]^{2}}-\frac{\mathbf{Cov}_{t-1}\quantity[\npt_t,\npo_t^2]}{\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}^{2}]^{2}}

\displaystyle\geq\widetilde{\gamma}_{t-1}+\frac{\beta_{t-1}(1-\beta_{t-1})^{2}}{n\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]^{2}}-\frac{\E_{t-1}[\gamma_{t}]\beta_{t-1}}{n\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]^{4}}-\frac{4\E_{t-1}[\gamma_{t}]}{n\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]^{4}}

\displaystyle(\text{\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{claim:covariance of gamma and beta2}})

\displaystyle\geq\widetilde{\gamma}_{t-1}+\frac{1}{n\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]^{2}}\beta_{t-1}(1-\beta_{t-1})^{2}-\frac{50\gamma_{t-1}}{\quantity(\npo_{t-1}(1-\npo_{t-1})^2-\frac{50\npt_{t-1}}{\E_{t-1}[\npo_t]^2})_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]^{2}}

\displaystyle(\text{\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:basic inequalities for gamma} (\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{item:expectation of gamma}}))

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\gamma}_{t-1}+\frac{1}{n\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]^{2}}\quantity(\frac{\npo_{t-1}}{4}\qty(1-\frac{\psi_{t-1}+\gamma_{t-1}}{\beta_{t-1}})^2-\frac{50\npt_{t-1}}{(\beta_{t-1}-\psi_{t-1})^2}).

Note that
1-\beta_{t-1}=\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\psi_{t-1}+\gamma_{t-1}}{2\beta_{t-1}}
holds from the definition of
\psi_{t}=\beta_{t}(2\beta_{t}-1)-\gamma_{t}
.
From the assumption that
\psi_{t-1},\gamma_{t-1}\leq o(1)
, and
\beta_{t-1}\geq 1/2-o(1)
, we obtain the claim.

Claim B.6
.

For any
t\geq 1
, we have

\mathbf{Cov}_{t-1}\quantity[\gamma_t,\beta_t^2]\leq\frac{4\E_{t-1}[\gamma_{t}]}{n}
.

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle\gamma_{t}=\sum_{i\in[k]}\quantity(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{v\in V}\indicator_{\opn_t(v)=i})^{2}=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{v\in V}\sum_{u\in V}\mathbf{1}_{\mathrm{opn}_{t}(v)=\mathrm{opn}_{t}(u)}\mathbf{1}_{\mathrm{opn}_{t}(v)\neq\bot},

(42)

\displaystyle\beta_{t}^{2}=\quantity(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{v\in V}\indicator_{\opn_t(v)=\bot})^{2}=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{v\in V}\sum_{u\in V}\mathbf{1}_{\mathrm{opn}_{t}(v)\neq\bot}\mathbf{1}_{\mathrm{opn}_{t}(u)\neq\bot}.

(43)

Hence,

\displaystyle\Cov_{t-1}\quantity[\gamma_t,\beta_t^2]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{4}}\sum_{v,u,a,b\in V}\Cov_{t-1}\quantity[\indicator_{\opn_t(v)=\opn_t(u)}\indicator_{\opn_t(v)\neq\bot},\indicator_{\opn_t(a)\neq\bot}\indicator_{\opn_t(b)\neq\bot}]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{4}}\sum_{v,u,a,b\in V:\{v,u\}\cap\{a,b\}\neq\emptyset}\Cov_{t-1}\quantity[\indicator_{\opn_t(v)=\opn_t(u)}\indicator_{\opn_t(v)\neq\bot},\indicator_{\opn_t(a)\neq\bot}\indicator_{\opn_t(b)\neq\bot}]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n^{4}}\sum_{v,u,a,b\in V:\{v,u\}\cap\{a,b\}\neq\emptyset}\E_{t-1}\quantity[\indicator_{\opn_t(v)=\opn_t(u)}\indicator_{\opn_t(v)\neq\bot}\indicator_{\opn_t(a)\neq\bot}\indicator_{\opn_t(b)\neq\bot}]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n^{4}}\sum_{v,u\in V}\sum_{a,b\in V:\{v,u\}\cap\{a,b\}\neq\emptyset}\E_{t-1}\quantity[\indicator_{\opn_t(v)=\opn_t(u)}\indicator_{\opn_t(v)\neq\bot}]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4n}{n^{2}}\E_{t-1}\quantity[\gamma_t].

∎

∎

B.2
Population Protocol Model

Proof of
Lemma

4.9
.

(i) Expectation.

First, observe that, for any
i\in[k]
and
t\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\alpha_{t}(i)-\alpha_{t-1}(i)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{n}&\text{with probability}\;(1-\beta_{t-1})\alpha_{t-1}(i),\\
-\frac{1}{n}&\text{with probability}\;\alpha_{t-1}(i)(\beta_{t-1}-\alpha_{t-1}(i)),\\
0&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

(44)

Hence, we have

\displaystyle\E_{t-1}[\alpha_{t}(i)]

\displaystyle=\alpha_{t-1}(i)+\frac{1}{n}(1-\beta_{t-1})\alpha_{t-1}(i)-\frac{1}{n}\alpha_{t-1}(i)(\beta_{t-1}-\alpha_{t-1}(i))

\displaystyle=\alpha_{t-1}(i)\quantity(1+\frac{\alpha_{t-1}(i)+1-2\beta_{t-1}}{n}).

(ii) Variance.

From
\Var_{t-1}[\alpha_{t}(i)]=\Var_{t-1}\quantity[\alpha_{t}(i)-\alpha_{t-1}(i)]
and
Eq.

44
, we have

\displaystyle\Var_{t-1}[\alpha_{t}(i)]

\displaystyle=\E_{t-1}\quantity[\qty(\alpha_{t}(i)-\alpha_{t-1}(i))^2]-\E_{t-1}\quantity[\alpha_{t}(i)-\alpha_{t-1}(i)]^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha_{t}(i)\quantity(1-\alpha_{t-1}(i))}{n^{2}}-\frac{\alpha_{t-1}(i)^{2}}{n^{2}}\quantity(1-2\beta_{t-1}+\alpha_{t-1}(i))^{2}.

(iii) Bernstein condition.

First,
\alpha_{t}(i)-\E_{t-1}[\alpha_{t}(i)]
satisfies
\frac{1}{n},\quantity(\frac{1}{n},\Var_{t-1}[\alpha_t(i)])_{t-1}[\alpha_{t}(i)]
-Bernstein condition by
Lemma

3.7
(
Item

1
).
Further, we have
\Var_{t-1}[\alpha_{t}(i)]\leq\frac{\alpha_{t-1}(i)}{n}
from
Item

2
of
Lemma

4.2
.
Hence, from
Lemma

3.7
(
Item

2
), we obtain the claim.
∎

Proof of
Lemma

4.10
.

(i) Expectation.
By definition,

\displaystyle\beta_{t}-\beta_{t-1}=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{n}&\text{with probability }\;(1-\beta_{t-1})\beta_{t-1},\\
-\frac{1}{n}&\text{with probability }\;\sum_{i\in[k]}\alpha_{t-1}(i)\quantity(\beta_{t-1}-\alpha_{t-1}(i))=\beta_{t-1}^{2}-\gamma_{t-1},\\
0&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

(45)

Thus, we obtain

\displaystyle\E_{t-1}\quantity[\beta_t]

\displaystyle=\beta_{t-1}+\frac{(1-\beta_{t-1})\beta_{t-1}}{n}-\frac{\beta_{t-1}^{2}-\gamma_{t-1}}{n}

\displaystyle=\beta_{t-1}+\frac{\beta_{t-1}\quantity(1-2\beta_{t-1})+\gamma_{t-1}}{n}.

(ii) Variance.

From
\Var_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]=\Var_{t-1}\quantity[\beta_{t}-\beta_{t-1}]
and
Eq.

45
, we have

\displaystyle\Var_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]

\displaystyle\leq\E_{t-1}\quantity[\qty(\beta_{t}-\beta_{t-1})^2]=\frac{(1-\beta_{t-1})\beta_{t-1}+\beta_{t-1}^{2}-\gamma_{t-1}}{n^{2}}=\frac{\beta_{t-1}-\gamma_{t-1}}{n^{2}}.

(46)

(iii) Bernstein condition.

From
Lemma

3.7
(
Item

1
),

\beta_{t}-\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]
satisfies
\frac{1}{n},\quantity(\frac{1}{n},\Var_{t-1}[\beta_t])_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]
-Bernstein condition.
Hence, from
Lemma

3.7
(
Item

2
) and
Lemma

4.10
(
Item

2
), we obtain the claim.

∎

Proof of
Lemma

4.11
.

(i) Expectation.

From
Lemma

4.9
(
Item

1
), we have

\displaystyle\E_{t-1}\quantity[\delta_t^{(\epsilon)}]

\displaystyle=\delta_{t-1}^{(\varepsilon)}\quantity(1+\frac{\alpha_{t-1}(i)+\alpha_{t-1}(j)+1-2\beta_{t-1}}{n})+\frac{\varepsilon\alpha_{t-1}(i)\alpha_{t-1}(j)}{n}.

(ii) Variance.

Form
Lemma

4.11
(
Item

1
), we have

\displaystyle\Var_{t-1}\quantity[\delta_t]

\displaystyle=\E_{t-1}\quantity[\qty(\delta_t-\delta_{t-1})^2]-\E_{t-1}\quantity[\delta_t-\delta_{t-1}]^{2}

\displaystyle=\E_{t-1}\quantity[\qty(\alpha_t(i)-\alpha_{t-1}(i))^2]+\E_{t-1}\quantity[\qty(\alpha_t(j)-\alpha_{t-1}(j))^2]-\E_{t-1}\quantity[\delta_t-\delta_{t-1}]^{2}

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha_{t-1}(i)\quantity(1-\alpha_{t-1}(i))}{n^{2}}+\frac{\alpha_{t-1}(j)\quantity(1-\alpha_{t-1}(j))}{n^{2}}-\frac{\delta_{t-1}^{2}}{n^{2}}\quantity(1-2\beta_{t-1}+\alpha_{t-1}(i)+\alpha_{t-1}(j))^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\alpha_{t-1}(i)\quantity(1-\alpha_{t-1}(i))}{n^{2}}+\frac{\alpha_{t-1}(j)\quantity(1-\alpha_{t-1}(j))}{n^{2}}-\frac{4\delta_{t-1}^{2}}{n^{2}}.

(iii) Bernstein condition.

Since

\displaystyle\delta_{t}^{(\varepsilon)}-\E_{t-1}\quantity[\delta_t^{(\epsilon)}]=\alpha_{t}(i)-\quantity(\alpha_t(i)-\E_{t-1}\qty[\alpha_t(i)])_{t-1}\quantity[\alpha_t(i)]-(1+\varepsilon)\alpha_{t}(j)-\quantity(\alpha_t(j)-\E_{t-1}\qty[\alpha_t(j)])_{t-1}\quantity[\alpha_t(j)],

applying
Lemma

3.8
(
Item

1
) and
Lemma

4.9
(
Item

3
),

\delta_{t}^{(\varepsilon)}-\E_{t-1}\quantity[\delta_t^{(\epsilon)}]
satisfies
\quantity(\frac{2(1+\epsilon)}{n},s)
-Bernstein condition for
s=\frac{2}{n^{2}}\quantity(\alpha_{t-1}(i)+(1+\epsilon)^2\alpha_{t-1}(j))
.
∎

Proof of
Lemma

4.12
.

Suppose that
v\in V
chooses
u\in V
at time
t-1
.
Then,

\displaystyle\gamma_{t}-\gamma_{t-1}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}-\frac{2\alpha_{t-1}(i)}{n}+\frac{1}{n^{2}}&\text{if $\mathrm{opn}_{t-1}(v)=i$ and $\mathrm{opn}_{t-1}(u)=j$ for $i\neq j$},\\
\frac{2\alpha_{t-1}(i)}{n}+\frac{1}{n^{2}}&\text{if $\mathrm{opn}_{t-1}(v)=\bot$ and $\mathrm{opn}_{t-1}(u)=i$},\\
0&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

(47)

Indeed, for the first case, we have

\displaystyle\gamma_{t}-\gamma_{t-1}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{t}(i)^{2}-\alpha_{t-1}(i)^{2}=\quantity(\alpha_{t-1}(i)-\frac{1}{n})^{2}-\alpha_{t-1}(i)^{2}=-\frac{2\alpha_{t-1}(i)}{n}+\frac{1}{n^{2}},

and for the second case, we have

\displaystyle\gamma_{t}-\gamma_{t-1}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{t}(i)^{2}-\alpha_{t-1}(i)^{2}=\quantity(\alpha_{t-1}(i)+\frac{1}{n})^{2}-\alpha_{t-1}(i)^{2}=\frac{2\alpha_{t-1}(i)}{n}+\frac{1}{n^{2}}.

Note that
Eq.

47
implies the following fact: For any
t\geq 1
, we have

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{\gamma_t-\gamma_{t-1}}\leq\frac{1}{n^{2}}+\frac{2\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}}{n}\leq\frac{3\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}}{n}.

(48)

Here, we use the fact that
\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}\leq\frac{1}{n}
holds (if this does not hold, then
\gamma_{t}-\gamma_{t-1}=0
and
Eq.

48
holds trivially).

(i) Expectation.

From
Eq.

47
,

\displaystyle\E_{t-1}\quantity[\gamma_t-\gamma_{t-1}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in[k]}\sum_{j\in[k]\setminus\{i\}}\quantity(\frac{1}{n^2}-\frac{2\alpha_{t-1}(i)}{n})\alpha_{t-1}(i)\alpha_{t-1}(j)+\sum_{i\in[k]}\quantity(\frac{1}{n^2}+\frac{2\alpha_{t-1}(i)}{n})(1-\beta_{t-1})\alpha_{t-1}(i)

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i\in[k]}\alpha_{t-1}(i)^{2}\quantity(1-2\beta_{t-1}+\alpha_{t-1}(i))+\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i\in[k]}\alpha_{t-1}(i)\quantity(1-\alpha_{t-1}(i))

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{n}\quantity((1-2\beta_{t-1})\gamma_{t-1}+\norm{\alpha_{t-1}}_3^3)+\frac{\beta_{t-1}-\gamma_{t-1}}{n^{2}}

holds and we obtain the claim.

(ii) Variance.

Note that
\Var_{t-1}[\gamma_{t}]=\Var_{t-1}[\gamma_{t}-\gamma_{t-1}]\leq\E_{t-1}[(\gamma_{t}-\gamma_{t-1})^{2}]
.
From
Eq.

47
, we have

\displaystyle\E_{t-1}\quantity[\qty(\gamma_t-\gamma_{t-1})^2]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in[k]}\sum_{j\in[k]\setminus\{i\}}\quantity(\frac{1}{n^2}-\frac{2\alpha_{t-1}(i)}{n})^{2}\alpha_{t-1}(i)\alpha_{t-1}(j)+\sum_{i\in[k]}\quantity(\frac{1}{n^2}+\frac{2\alpha_{t-1}(i)}{n})^{2}(1-\beta_{t-1})\alpha_{t-1}(i)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{4}}\sum_{i\in[k]}\alpha_{t-1}(i)\quantity(1-\alpha_{t-1}(i))+\frac{4}{n^{2}}\sum_{i\in[k]}\alpha_{t-1}(i)^{3}\quantity(1-\alpha_{t-1}(i))

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+\frac{4}{n^{3}}\sum_{i\in[k]}\alpha_{t-1}(i)^{2}\quantity(1-2\beta_{t-1}+\alpha_{t-1}(i))

\displaystyle=\frac{\beta_{t-1}-\gamma_{t-1}}{n^{4}}+\frac{4}{n^{2}}\quantity(\norm{\alpha_{t-1}}_3^3-\norm{\alpha_{t-1}}_4^4)+\frac{4}{n^{3}}\quantity((1-2\beta_{t-1})\gamma_{t-1}+\norm{\alpha_{t-1}}_3^3).

Since

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in[k]}\alpha_{t-1}(i)^{h+\ell}=\sum_{i\in[k]:\alpha_{t-1}(i)>0}\alpha_{t-1}(i)^{h+\ell}\geq\frac{1}{n^{\ell}}\sum_{i\in[k]:\alpha_{t-1}(i)>0}\alpha_{t-1}(i)^{h}=\frac{1}{n^{\ell}}\sum_{i\in[k]}\alpha_{t-1}(i)^{h}

holds for any
h,\ell\geq 1
,
we have

\norm{\alpha_{t-1}}_{3}^{3}\leq n\norm{\alpha_{t-1}}_{4}^{4}
,

\gamma_{t-1}\leq n\norm{\alpha_{t-1}}_{3}^{3}
, and

\beta_{t-1}\leq n^{2}\norm{\alpha_{t-1}}_{3}^{3}
.
Hence, we obtain the claim.

(iii) Bernstein condition.

From
Eq.

48
, we have

\displaystyle\gamma_{t}-\absolutevalue{\gamma_t-\E_{t-1}[\gamma_t]}_{t-1}[\gamma_{t}]=\gamma_{t}-\gamma_{t-1}-\absolutevalue{\gamma_t-\gamma_{t-1}-\E_{t-1}[\gamma_t-\gamma_{t-1}]}_{t-1}[\gamma_{t}-\gamma_{t-1}]\leq 2\absolutevalue{\gamma_t-\gamma_{t-1}}\leq\frac{6\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}}{n}.

(49)

Hence, from
Lemma

3.7
(
Item

1
),

\gamma_{t}-\E_{t-1}[\gamma_{t}]
satisfies
\frac{6\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}}{n},\quantity(\frac{6\npm_{t-1}}{n},\Var_{t-1}[\gamma_t])_{t-1}[\gamma_{t}]
-Bernstein condition.
Combining this with
Lemma

3.7
(
Item

2
) and
Lemma

4.12
(
Item

2
), we obtain the claim.
∎

Proof of
Lemma

4.13
.

(i) Expectation.

First, from
Lemma

4.12
(
Item

1
), we have

\displaystyle\beta_{t-1}\E_{t-1}[\gamma_{t}-\gamma_{t-1}]

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{n}\quantity(\beta_{t-1}(1-2\beta_{t-1})\gamma_{t-1}+\beta_{t-1}\norm{\alpha_{t-1}}_3^3)+\beta_{t-1}\frac{\beta_{t-1}-\gamma_{t-1}}{n^{2}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{2}{n}\quantity(\beta_{t-1}(1-2\beta_{t-1})\gamma_{t-1}+\gamma_{t-1}^2)+\beta_{t-1}\frac{\beta_{t-1}-\gamma_{t-1}}{n^{2}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{2\gamma_{t-1}}{n}\psi_{t-1}+\beta_{t-1}\frac{\beta_{t-1}-\gamma_{t-1}}{n^{2}}.

Note that
\gamma_{t-1}^{2}\leq\beta_{t-1}\norm{\alpha_{t-1}}_{3}^{3}
holds from the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality.
Hence, from
Eqs.

45
and
46
,

\displaystyle\beta_{t-1}\E_{t-1}\quantity[\psi_t-\psi_{t-1}]

\displaystyle=2\beta_{t-1}\E_{t-1}\quantity[\beta_t^2-\beta_{t-1}^2]-\beta_{t-1}\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}-\beta_{t-1}]-\beta_{t-1}\E_{t-1}[\gamma_{t}-\gamma_{t-1}]

\displaystyle=2\beta_{t-1}\E_{t-1}\quantity[\qty(\beta_t-\beta_{t-1})^2]+\beta_{t-1}(4\beta_{t-1}-1)\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}-\beta_{t-1}]-\beta_{t-1}\E_{t-1}[\gamma_{t}-\gamma_{t-1}]

\displaystyle\leq 2\beta_{t-1}\frac{\beta_{t-1}-\gamma_{t-1}}{n^{2}}-\beta_{t-1}(4\beta_{t-1}-1)\frac{\psi_{t-1}}{n}+\frac{2\gamma_{t-1}}{n}\psi_{t-1}-\beta_{t-1}\frac{\beta_{t-1}-\gamma_{t-1}}{n^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\psi_{t-1}}{n}\quantity(-2\psi_{t-1}-\beta_{t-1})+\beta_{t-1}\frac{\beta_{t-1}-\gamma_{t-1}}{n^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{\psi_{t-1}}{n}\beta_{t-1}+\beta_{t-1}\frac{\beta_{t-1}-\gamma_{t-1}}{n^{2}}

holds and we obtain the claim.

(ii) Bernstein condition.

From definition,

\psi_{t}-\E_{t-1}[\psi_{t}]=2\beta_{t}^{2}-\quantity(\beta_t^2-\E_{t-1}[\beta_t^2])_{t-1}[\beta_{t}^{2}]-\beta_{t}-\quantity(\beta_t-\E_{t-1}[\beta_t])_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]-\gamma_{t}-\quantity(\gamma_t-\E_{t-1}[\gamma_t])_{t-1}[\gamma_{t}].

In what follows, we show that
\beta_{t}^{2}-\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}^{2}]
satisfies
\quantity(\frac{16}{n},\frac{36\beta_{t-1}^4}{n^2})
-Bernstein condition.
If this holds, then by applying
Lemma

3.7
(
Item

2
), together with
Lemma

4.10
(
Item

3
) and
Lemma

4.12
(
Item

3
), the claim follows.

First, we have

\displaystyle\beta_{t}^{2}-\absolutevalue{\beta_t^2-\E_{t-1}[\beta_t^2]}_{t-1}[\beta_{t}^{2}]=\beta_{t}^{2}-\beta_{t-1}^{2}-\absolutevalue{\beta_t^2-\beta_{t-1}^2-\E_{t-1}[\beta_t^2-\beta_{t-1}^2]}_{t-1}[\beta_{t}^{2}-\beta_{t-1}^{2}]\leq 2\absolutevalue{\beta_t^2-\beta_{t-1}^2}\leq 4\absolutevalue{\beta_t-\beta_{t-1}}\leq\frac{4}{n}.

Now, we show that
\Var_{t-1}[\beta_{t}^{2}]\leq\frac{9\beta_{t-1}^{4}}{n^{2}}
.
If
\beta_{t-1}=0
, then
\beta_{t}^{2}-\beta_{t-1}^{2}=0
and the inequality holds.
Suppose
v\in V
chooses
u\in V
at time
t-1
.
If
\mathrm{opn}_{t-1}(v)=i
and
\mathrm{opn}_{t-1}(u)=j
for
i\neq j
, we have

\displaystyle\beta_{t}^{2}-\beta_{t-1}^{2}=-\frac{2\beta_{t-1}}{n}+\frac{1}{n^{2}}.

(50)

If
\mathrm{opn}_{t-1}(v)=\bot
and
\mathrm{opn}_{t-1}(u)=i
, we have

\displaystyle\beta_{t}^{2}-\beta_{t-1}^{2}=\frac{2\beta_{t-1}}{n}+\frac{1}{n^{2}}.

(51)

Otherwise, we have
\beta_{t}^{2}-\beta_{t-1}^{2}=0
.
Thus, we obtain

\displaystyle\E_{t-1}\quantity[\qty(\beta_t^2-\beta_{t-1}^2)^2]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in[k]}\sum_{j\in[k]\setminus\{i\}}\quantity(\frac{1}{n^2}-\frac{2\beta_{t-1}}{n})^{2}\alpha_{t-1}(i)\alpha_{t-1}(j)+\sum_{i\in[k]}\quantity(\frac{1}{n^2}+\frac{2\beta_{t-1}}{n})^{2}(1-\beta_{t-1})\alpha_{t-1}(i)

\displaystyle=\quantity(\frac{1}{n^2}-\frac{2\beta_{t-1}}{n})^{2}\quantity(\beta_{t-1}^2-\gamma_{t-1})+\quantity(\frac{1}{n^2}+\frac{2\beta_{t-1}}{n})^{2}\beta_{t-1}\quantity(1-\beta_{t-1})

\displaystyle=\quantity(\frac{1}{n^4}+\frac{4\beta_{t-1}^2}{n^2})\quantity(\beta_{t-1}^2-\gamma_{t-1})+\frac{4\beta_{t-1}^{2}}{n^{3}}\quantity(\beta_{t-1}(1-2\beta_{t-1})+\gamma_{t-1})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{9\beta_{t-1}^{4}}{n^{2}}.

Note that
\Var_{t-1}[\beta_{t}^{2}]=\Var_{t-1}[\beta_{t}^{2}-\beta_{t-1}^{2}]\leq\E_{t-1}[\quantity(\beta_t^2-\beta_{t-1}^2)^{2}]
.
Hence, from
Lemma

3.7
(
Item

1
), we obtain the claim.
∎

Proof of
Lemma

4.14
.

(i) Expectation.

From
Lemma

B.1
(
Item

1
), we have

\displaystyle\E_{t-1}\quantity[\tnpm_t]\geq\E_{t-1}\quantity[\tnp_t(I_{t-1})]\geq\frac{\E_{t-1}\quantity[\alpha_t(I_{t-1})]}{\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]}-\frac{\mathbf{Cov}_{t-1}\quantity[\alpha_t(I_{t-1}),\beta_t]}{\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]^{2}}.

From
Lemma

4.9
(
Item

1
) and
Lemma

4.10
(
Item

1
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{\E_{t-1}\quantity[\alpha_t(I_{t-1})]}{\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]}

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha_{t-1}(I_{t-1})}{\beta_{t-1}}1+\frac{\frac{\quantity(1+\frac{\frac{\E_{t-1}[\alpha_t(I_{t-1})]}{\alpha_{t-1}(I_{t-1})}-\frac{\E_{t-1}[\beta_t]}{\beta_{t-1}}}{\frac{\E_{t-1}[\beta_t]}{\beta_{t-1}}})_{t-1}[\alpha_{t}(I_{t-1})]}{\alpha_{t-1}(I_{t-1})}-\frac{\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]}{\beta_{t-1}}}{\frac{\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]}{\beta_{t-1}}}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}1+\frac{\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}-\gamma_{t-1}/\beta_{t-1}}{n\quantity(1+\frac{\npm_{t-1}-\gamma_{t-1}/\beta_{t-1}}{n\E_{t-1}[\beta_t]/\beta_{t-1}})_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]/\beta_{t-1}}

\displaystyle\geq\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}\quantity(1+\frac{\npm_{t-1}-\gamma_{t-1}/\beta_{t-1}}{2n}).

(52)

Note that
\gamma_{t-1}\leq\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}\beta_{t-1}
holds.

Now, we complete the proof by showing that
\mathbf{Cov}_{t-1}\quantity[\alpha_t(I_{t-1}),\beta_t]\leq\frac{2\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}}{n^{2}}
.
To see this, from
Lemma

4.9
(
Item

2
) and
Lemma

4.10
(
Item

1
), we have

\displaystyle\Cov_{t-1}\quantity[\alpha_{t}(i),\beta_{t}]

\displaystyle=\Cov_{t-1}\quantity[\alpha_t(i)-\alpha_{t-1}(i),\beta_{t-1}-\beta_{t-1}]

\displaystyle=\E_{t-1}\quantity[\qty(\alpha_t(i)-\alpha_{t-1}(i))\qty(\beta_t-\beta_{t-1})]-\E_{t-1}\quantity[\alpha_t(i)-\alpha_{t-1}(i)]\E_{t-1}\quantity[\beta_t-\beta_{t-1}]

\displaystyle=\E_{t-1}\quantity[\qty(\alpha_t(i)-\alpha_{t-1}(i))^2]-\E_{t-1}\quantity[\alpha_t(i)-\alpha_{t-1}(i)]\E_{t-1}\quantity[\beta_t-\beta_{t-1}]

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha_{t-1}(i)}{n^{2}}\quantity(1-\alpha_{t-1}(i)-\qty(1-2\beta_{t-1}+\alpha_{t-1}(i))\qty(\beta_{t-1}(1-2\beta_{t-1})+\gamma_{t-1})))

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\alpha_{t-1}(i)}{n^{2}}.

Hence,

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbf{Cov}_{t-1}\quantity[\alpha_t(I_{t-1}),\beta_t]}{\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]^{2}}\leq\frac{18\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}}{n^{2}}

(53)

and we obtain the claim.
Note that we use assumptions of
\beta_{t-1}\geq 1/2-o(1)
,
and
\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]\geq\beta_{t-1}-1/n\geq 1/3
.

(ii) Bernstein condition.

From
Lemma

B.1
(
Item

2
),
Lemmas

4.10
,
53
and
52
, we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}\quantity(1+\frac{\npm_{t-1}-\gamma_{t-1}/\beta_{t-1}}{2n})-\widetilde{\alpha}^{\mathrm{max}}_{t}-\frac{18\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}}{n^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\E_{t-1}\quantity[\alpha_t(I_{t-1})]}{\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]}-\alpha_{t}(I_{t-1})-\frac{\Cov_{t-1}\quantity[\alpha_t(I_{t-1}),\beta_t]}{\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\quantity(\E_{t-1}\qty[\alpha_t(I_{t-1})]-\alpha_{t-1}(I_{t-1}))_{t-1}\quantity[\alpha_t(I_{t-1})]-\alpha_{t-1}(I_{t-1})}{\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]}-\frac{\alpha_{t}(I_{t-1})\beta_{t}-\E_{t-1}\quantity[\alpha_t(I_{t-1})\beta_t]}{\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]^{2}}.

From
Lemma

4.9
(
Item

3
), the former term
\frac{2\quantity(\E_{t-1}\qty[\alpha_t(I_{t-1})]-\alpha_{t-1}(I_{t-1}))_{t-1}\quantity[\alpha_t(I_{t-1})]-\alpha_{t-1}(I_{t-1})}{\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]}
satisfies
\frac{2}{n\quantity(\frac{2}{n\E_{t-1}[\beta_t]},\frac{4\npm_{t-1}}{n^2\E_{t-1}[\beta_t]^2})_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]},\frac{4\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}}{n^{2}\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]^{2}}
-Bernstein condition.

Now, we show that
\frac{\alpha_{t}(I_{t-1})\beta_{t}-\E_{t-1}\quantity[\alpha_t(I_{t-1})\beta_t]}{\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]^{2}}
satisfies the
\frac{6\beta_{t-1}}{n\quantity(\frac{6\beta_{t-1}}{n\E_{t-1}[\beta_t]^2},\frac{20\npm_{t-1}\beta_{t-1}}{n^2\E_{t-1}[\beta_t]^4})_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]^{2}},\frac{20\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}\beta_{t-1}}{n^{2}\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]^{4}}
-Bernstein condition.
Once this is established, applying
Lemma

3.8
(
Item

1
) and
Lemma

3.7
(
Item

1
) with
\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]\geq\beta_{t-1}-1/n\geq 1/3
completes the proof.

First, we have

\displaystyle\alpha_{t}(i)\beta_{t}-\absolutevalue{\alpha_t(i)\beta_t-\E_{t-1}\qty[\alpha_{t}(i)\beta_{t}]}_{t-1}\quantity[\alpha_{t}(i)\beta_{t}]

\displaystyle\leq 2\absolutevalue{\alpha_t(i)\beta_t-\alpha_{t-1}(i)\beta_{t-1}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\alpha_{t}(i)\absolutevalue{\beta_t(i)-\beta_{t-1}(i)}+2\beta_{t-1}\absolutevalue{\alpha_t(i)-\alpha_{t-1}(i)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{6\beta_{t-1}}{n}.

Furthermore, we have

\displaystyle\Var_{t-1}\quantity[\alpha_t(i)\beta_t]=\Var_{t-1}\quantity[\alpha_t(i)\beta_t-\alpha_{t-1}(i)\beta_{t-1}]\leq\E_{t-1}\quantity[\qty(\alpha_t(i)\beta_t - \alpha_{t-1}(i)\beta_{t-1})^2]

and

\displaystyle\E_{t-1}\quantity[\qty(\alpha_t(i)\beta_t - \alpha_{t-1}(i)\beta_{t-1})^2]

\displaystyle=\quantity(\frac{\alpha_{t-1}(i)}{n}+\frac{\beta_{t-1}}{n}+\frac{1}{n^2})^{2}\quantity(1-\beta_{t-1})\alpha_{t-1}(i)

\displaystyle+\quantity(\frac{\alpha_{t-1}(i)}{n})^{2}(1-\beta_{t-1})\quantity(\beta_{t-1}-\alpha_{t-1}(i))

\displaystyle+\quantity(-\frac{\alpha_{t-1}(i)}{n}-\frac{\beta_{t-1}}{n}+\frac{1}{n^2})^{2}\alpha_{t-1}(i)\quantity(\beta_{t-1}-\alpha_{t-1}(i))

\displaystyle+\quantity(-\frac{\alpha_{t-1}(i)}{n})^{2}\sum_{j\in[k]\setminus\{i\}}\alpha_{t-1}(j)\quantity(\beta_{t-1}-\alpha_{t-1}(j))

\displaystyle\leq\frac{20\alpha_{t-1}(i)\beta_{t-1}}{n^{2}},

and we obtain the claim from
Lemma

3.7
(
Item

1
).
∎

Proof of
Lemma

4.15
.

Write
x=\frac{1}{n}\quantity(1-2\beta_{t-1}+\frac{\gamma_{t-1}}{\beta_{t-1}})
for convenience.
Then, from
Lemma

4.10
(
Item

1
), we have

\displaystyle\E_{t-1}\quantity[\beta_t]=\beta_{t-1}+\frac{\beta_{t-1}\quantity(1-2\beta_{t-1})+\gamma_{t-1}}{n}=\beta_{t-1}(1+x),

and from
Lemma

4.12
(
Item

1
), we have

\displaystyle\E_{t-1}\quantity[\gamma_t]

\displaystyle\geq\gamma_{t-1}+\frac{2}{n}\quantity((1-2\beta_{t-1})\gamma_{t-1}+\frac{\gamma_{t-1}^2}{\beta_{t-1}})+\frac{\beta_{t-1}-\gamma_{t-1}}{n^{2}}=\gamma_{t-1}\quantity(1+2x)+\frac{\beta_{t-1}-\gamma_{t-1}}{n^{2}}.

Note that
\norm{\alpha_{t-1}}_{3}^{3}\geq\beta_{t-1}\left(\sum_{i\in[k]}\frac{\alpha_{t-1}(i)}{\beta_{t-1}}\right)^{2}=\gamma_{t-1}^{2}/\beta_{t-1}
holds from Jensen’s inequality.
Hence,

\displaystyle\frac{\E_{t-1}\quantity[\gamma_t]}{\E_{t-1}\quantity[\beta_t]^{2}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\gamma_{t-1}(1+2x)+\frac{\beta_{t-1}-\gamma_{t-1}}{n^{2}}}{\beta_{t-1}^{2}(1+x)^{2}}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\gamma}_{t-1}+\frac{\frac{\beta_{t-1}-\gamma_{t-1}}{n^{2}}-\gamma_{t-1}x^{2}}{\beta_{t-1}^{2}(1+x)^{2}}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\gamma}_{t-1}+\frac{\beta_{t-1}-\gamma_{t-1}-\gamma_{t-1}\quantity(1-2\beta_{t-1}+\frac{\gamma_{t-1}}{\beta_{t-1}})^{2}}{n^{2}\E_{t-1}\quantity[\beta_t]^{2}}.

Now, we give an upper bound of
\Cov_{t-1}\quantity[\gamma_t,\beta_t^2]
.
From
Eqs.

50
,
51
and
47
, we have

\displaystyle\E_{t-1}\quantity[\qty(\gamma_t-\gamma_{t-1})(\beta_t^2-\beta_{t-1}^2)]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in[k]}\sum_{j\in[k]\setminus\{i\}}\quantity(\frac{1}{n^2}-\frac{2\alpha_{t-1}(i)}{n})\quantity(\frac{1}{n^2}-\frac{2\beta_{t-1}}{n})\alpha_{t-1}(i)\alpha_{t-1}(j)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i\in[k]}\quantity(\frac{1}{n^2}+\frac{2\alpha_{t-1}(i)}{n})\quantity(\frac{1}{n^2}+\frac{2\beta_{t-1}}{n})(1-\beta_{t-1})\alpha_{t-1}(i)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{3}}\quantity(\frac{1}{n}-2\beta_{t-1})\quantity(\beta_{t-1}^2-\gamma_{t-1})-\frac{2}{n^{2}}\quantity(\frac{1}{n}-2\beta_{t-1})\quantity(\gamma_{t-1}\beta_{t-1}-\norm{\alpha_{t-1}}_3^3)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{n^{3}}\quantity(\frac{1}{n}+2\beta_{t-1})\beta_{t-1}\quantity(1-\beta_{t-1})+\frac{2}{n^{2}}\quantity(\frac{1}{n}+2\beta_{t-1})\gamma_{t-1}\quantity(1-\beta_{t-1})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{24\gamma_{t-1}}{n^{2}}.

Note that
\beta_{t-1}\geq 1/n
,
\gamma_{t-1}\geq\frac{\beta_{t-1}^{2}}{k}
, and
\norm{\alpha_{t-1}}_{3}^{3}\leq\gamma_{t-1}\alpha^{\mathrm{max}}_{t-1}
hold.
Furthermore, we have

\displaystyle\E_{t-1}\quantity[\beta_t^2-\beta_{t-1}^2]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in[k]}\sum_{j\in[k]\setminus\{i\}}\quantity(\frac{1}{n^2}-\frac{2\beta_{t-1}}{n})\alpha_{t-1}(i)\alpha_{t-1}(j)+\sum_{i\in[k]}\quantity(\frac{1}{n^2}+\frac{2\beta_{t-1}}{n})(1-\beta_{t-1})\alpha_{t-1}(i)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\quantity(\frac{1}{n}-2\beta_{t-1})\quantity(\beta_{t-1}^2-\gamma_{t-1})+\frac{1}{n}\quantity(\frac{1}{n}+2\beta_{t-1})\beta_{t-1}\quantity(1-\beta_{t-1})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{6\beta_{t-1}^{2}}{n}

and
\E_{t-1}\quantity[\gamma_t-\gamma_{t-1}]\leq\frac{5\gamma_{t-1}}{n\beta_{t-1}}
holds from
Lemma

4.12
(
Item

1
).
Hence,

\displaystyle\Cov_{t-1}\quantity[\gamma_t,\beta_t^2]

\displaystyle=\E_{t-1}\quantity[\gamma_t-\gamma_{t-1}]\E_{t-1}\quantity[\beta_t^2-\beta_{t-1}^2]-\E_{t-1}\quantity[\gamma_t-\gamma_{t-1}]\E_{t-1}\quantity[\beta_t^2-\beta_{t-1}^2]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{24\gamma_{t-1}}{n^{2}}+\frac{5\gamma_{t-1}}{n\beta_{t-1}}\cdot\frac{6\beta_{t-1}^{2}}{n}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{54\gamma_{t-1}}{n^{2}}.

Combining the above inequalities, we obtain

\displaystyle\E_{t-1}\quantity[\tnpt_t]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\E_{t-1}\quantity[\npt_t]}{\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}^{2}]}-\frac{\mathbf{Cov}_{t-1}\quantity[\npt_t,\npo_t^2]}{\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}^{2}]^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\E_{t-1}\quantity[\npt_t]}{\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]^{2}}-\frac{\E_{t-1}\quantity[\npt_t]\Var_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]}{\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}^{2}]\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}]^{2}}-\frac{\mathbf{Cov}_{t-1}\quantity[\npt_t,\npo_t^2]}{\E_{t-1}[\beta_{t}^{2}]^{2}}

\displaystyle\geq\widetilde{\gamma}_{t-1}+\frac{\beta_{t-1}}{n^{2}\E_{t-1}\quantity[\beta_t]^{2}}-\frac{5\gamma_{t-1}}{n^{2}\E_{t-1}\quantity[\beta_t]^{2}}-\frac{6\gamma_{t-1}\beta_{t-1}}{n^{2}\E_{t-1}\quantity[\beta_t]^{4}}-\frac{54\gamma_{t-1}}{n^{2}\E_{t-1}\quantity[\beta_t]^{4}}

\displaystyle\geq\widetilde{\gamma}_{t-1}+\frac{1}{6n^{2}}-\frac{80000\gamma_{t-1}}{n^{2}}

\displaystyle\geq\widetilde{\gamma}_{t-1}+\frac{1}{12n^{2}}.

Here, we use assumptions
\beta_{t-1}\geq 1/3
and
\gamma_{t-1}=o(1)
.
Note that
\beta_{t}\geq\beta_{t-1}-\frac{1}{n}\geq\frac{\beta_{t-1}}{2}\geq\frac{1}{6}
.

∎

Appendix C
First Round
\beta_{0}=1

In this section, we prove that in the gossip USD, the number of decided vertices after the first round becomes
\widetilde{O}(n\gamma_{0})
with high probability.

Lemma C.1
.

Consider Undecided State Dynamics in the gossip model with
\beta_{0}=1
.
Then, it holds with high probability that
\gamma_{0}-\sqrt{\gamma_{0}\log n/n}\leq\beta_{1}\leq\gamma_{0}\log n
.

Proof.

Since
\beta_{0}=1
, a vertex
u
is still decided after the first synchronous update if and only if it communicates with a vertex holding the same opinion as
u
, which occurs with probability
\alpha_{0}(i)
if
u
has opinion
i
.
Therefore, we have

\E[\beta_{1}]=\sum_{i\in[k]}\alpha_{0}(i)\cdot\alpha_{0}(i)=\gamma_{0}.

Moreover, the quantity
n\beta_{1}
is the sum of
n
independent random variables, each of which is bounded by
1
.
Therefore, by a variant of the Chernoff bound (
Lemma

A.1
) for
h=n\gamma_{0}\log n\geq 6\E[\beta_{1}]
(for all sufficiently large
n
), we have

\Pr\quantity[ n\beta_1 \ge n\gamma_0\log n ]\leq 2^{-n\gamma_{0}\log n}\leq n^{-\Omega(1)}.

∎

From this lemma, we know that the number of remaining opinions becomes at most
\gamma_{0}\log n
with high probability after the first round.

Corollary C.2
.

Consider Undecided State Dynamics in the gossip model with
\beta_{0}=1
.
Let
k^{\prime}
be the number of opinions that remain after the first round.
Then, with high probability,
k^{\prime}\leq\min\quantity{ k, n\gamma_0\log n}
.

Proof.

From
Lemma

C.1
, with high probability, we have
k^{\prime}\leq\min\{k,n\beta_{1}\}\leq\min\{k,n\gamma_{0}\log n\}
.
∎
